ParlaMint-ES_2020-04-09-CD200409.u1	Very good morning, ladies and gentlemen, the session is open. This is the third session of the plenary session of the Congress of Deputies that we are holding under the special conditions that are before us all, conditions whose sole purpose is to maintain the parliamentary function as long as we comply with the measures that have been implemented by the health emergency in which we find ourselves. Today's session will address the request for authorization of the extension of the state of alarm and the validation of several decree-laws related specifically also to the fight against COVID-19 and the mitigation of its effects. We will, as you know, do so not only in the Chamber but also in the other Members of the House, who will fulfil their obligation as parliamentarians following the sitting and voting telematically and with their duties as citizens doing so from their home. At the beginning of these sessions, I have always had words for the dead, the sick, the health workers, those who feed us, those who care for us and the rest of the workers who fight the pandemic in the front line, for those who are responsible for maintaining essential services, including the workers of Congress, and specifically for members of the Armed Forces and the State Security Forces and Bodies. Today I would like to add in the relationship of gratitude, recognition and tribute to all citizens who, while staying at home, are also fighting the disease in a very valuable way. I would like to express the House's thanks to all of them. Finally, I invite those present to remember the deceased as a result of this disease, keeping a minute of silence. [[The ladies and gentlemen present in the Chamber, stood up, kept a minute of silence.]] Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
ParlaMint-ES_2020-04-09-CD200409.u2	In accordance with Rule 68.1 of the Rules of Procedure and as agreed by the spokesmen, this Presidency shall submit to the plenary of the House the amendment of the agenda in order to exchange the order of debate for the validation or repeal of the actual decree-laws, so that the one appearing as item 4 of the agenda will be discussed first and then points 2 and 3 cumulatively. Does the House remember that? [[Consent]] The amendment to the agenda was therefore adopted. I should also like to inform you that the proposals concerning the request for authorisation of the extension of the state of alarm will be voted on at the end of agenda item 1. ACTS RELATING TO THE STATE OF ALARM, EXCEPTION AND SITE: - APPLICATION FOR THE AUTHORIZATION OF THE PROGRESS OF THE STATE OF ALARMA DECLARED AS A REAL MEASURE DECIDED BY COVID-19 OF 14 MARCH, DECLARING THE STATE OF ALARMA FOR THE MANAGEMENT OF THE SITUATION OF HEALTH CRISIS PROVIDED FOR BY COVID-19, PROROGED BY THE REAL DECIDED BY 476/2020 OF 27 MARCH. [[File number 091/000001]]
ParlaMint-ES_2020-04-09-CD200409.u3	We begin with the debate on the request for authorization to extend the state of alarm declared by Royal Decree 463/2020 of 14 March, for the management of the situation of health crisis caused by COVID-19, extended by Royal Decree 476/2020 of 27 March. In order to explain the reasons for the request for an extension, the President of the Government has the floor on behalf of the Government.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-04-09-CD200409.u4	Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. Thank you, Madam President. I would like to begin my speech, as always, with the excited memory of all the compatriots who have lost their lives because of the COVID-19. In these days, one of the things that we find most difficult to understand - we have said it in some other appearance - and that most shakes us is death in solitude: people who have gone without the embrace of their loved ones, without their last farewell. There is no doubt that nothing will make up for that pain, but we will give them as soon as possible the tribute they deserve. If they did not have the closeness of their relatives, they will have at least the tribute of society as a whole. I also want to send from this House all the forces and desires for recovery to those who are fighting in hospitals and their homes against the disease, here in Spain and also anywhere else in the world, because we will not be free of the virus until this pandemic has completely disappeared all over the planet. And that is making it possible, in heroic work, our great health professionals and a public health to which we owe everything and must give everything. I want to remember, of course, the more than 52,000 healed in our country today: they and they are part of our collective joy and hope, they are the symbol that we are getting it, that this war against the virus will be a total victory. And I do not want to fail to insist, ladies and gentlemen, that the drastic measures taken, followed with discipline, courage and determination by Spanish society staying at home, have served to save many lives, the lives of people who never contracted the disease because we stopped the contagions through confinement. Ladies and gentlemen, the global magnitude of this terrible pandemic is overwhelming, and there are the data; the speed of its spread is dizzying. The last time I came up to this rostrum on 25 March, the World Health Organization counted some 414,000 people infected and about 18,000 dead worldwide. Just two weeks later, we have long exceeded 1,400 000 infected across the planet and the coronavirus has ended the lives of more than 80 000 people in the world. The global dimension is an indisputable fact and also the challenge for humanity as a whole. There is no longer any doubt about this: we have faced the greatest threat to the public health of the planet since the flu of 1918. Therefore, these are extremely extraordinary times that require exceptional measures, measures such as the one that calls us back to plenary today: the expansion of the state of alarm. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear again in this House to ask that all the parliamentary forces present here support the Royal Decree extending the current state of alarm adopted by the Government of Spain in the last Council of Ministers. It is, as you know, the second extension that we requested after the first Royal Decree published in the State Official Gazette on 14 March, a measure, in my view, in our view, essential, which received the support of the majority of the political parties represented here and which was again endorsed on 25 March. Support that we once again seek with the assurance that it remains an essential measure to protect the lives and health of our fellow citizens. We have unquestionable evidence, ladies and gentlemen, and the state of alarm is working. After weeks of a struggle that has been agonizing for moments, we see how the fire that triggered the pandemic begins to be under control. The data, logically, with all the prudence we can express, are encouraging. We have reached and surpassed the peak of COVID-19 contagions and we find ourselves near the beginning of the descent, which is called descalation. After the first stage of this emergency we begin the second, which is to bend the curve. And when we decreed the state of alarm, ladies and gentlemen, the average of contagion in Spain was around 22%; today the contagion is 4%. Then we had to make a decision for all of Spain, for the whole country, for all the autonomous communities, and that decision has been positive for all, but above all it has been beneficial for all citizens; live where they live, think what they think, we focus on people, because, ladies and gentlemen, the virus does not understand ideologies or territories. Your support then made it possible, ladies and gentlemen; it has made it possible by working exemplaryly all the representatives and also the public administrations of our country, regardless of their political sign, and above all it has made possible the exceptional sense of community of the whole of Spanish society that assumed the leadership of the experts and the health authorities and followed their instructions and recommendations with responsibility and also with social discipline. We are all aware of the enormous sacrifice that this second extension will entail for a citizen who, logically, already resents the effort made over the last month, but we also know that it is essential to consolidate what we have achieved with so much pain and suffering in this time frame, and that is something that we will only achieve if we keep the state of alarm until scientists consider it so. Therefore, our priority now is not to go astray, not to go back under any concept to the starting point, not to lower the guard. We begin to see the end of this long road that will lead us back to a new normality, and we have to make this new effort to get out of that tunnel. Ladies and gentlemen, as we know, Spain has reached the peak of maximum tension of this emergency and for a few days now we have begun to bend the contagion curve. If the ascent has been hard, it will not be less hard the descent, which also contains new dangers that we will have to draw among all. The return will have to be progressive, we will need an adaptation before returning to normality and we cannot even know with precision what kind of normality we will recover, because possibly there will be many aspects of our economic system, of our social system and even of our way of living that will have to be reformulated to be stronger in the face of an emergency like the one we suffer. But that will come later, ladies and gentlemen, although we have been working on it for some time. Soon it will touch on the so-called descaled, a gradual transition or return that has two objectives, in our opinion: the first of them is to continue to protect non-immunized citizens against the virus while the vaccine logically arrives; the second, to channel the progressive return towards the reconstruction of what we have called the economic and social plane. The impact of this crisis on our economic system is leading, among other issues, to loss of employment and destruction of our productive fabric. Complete reconstruction is a future phase that can only begin when we are safe from everything, but in order to reach it we must plan in parallel the second phase, that is, the phase of descalation. As I have already announced, the Government of Spain is preparing a plan so that this phase can be developed effectively, and in this planning there is a multidisciplinary team, composed of epidemiologists, technologists and specialists of the highest level in different disciplines that will be affected logically by this transition; a team that is designing the progressive reactivation of economic and social activity, with possible individual and collective measures that will be developed subject to the criteria of public health and also with the maximum collaboration and cooperation, as I told the last Conference of Presidents to the autonomous governments and also to the municipal governments. This plan, ladies and gentlemen, will be adapted to the evolution of the pandemic, both in our country and in the rest of the world. The measures covered will be hygiene, both individually and collectively; health screening and care for people infected with COVID-19; and thirdly, initiatives that necessarily have to do with the technological field of control and monitoring of the pandemic. This transition plan will be phased out, ladies and gentlemen, and its speed will depend on progress in the control of the pandemic. Scientists, technicians who advise the Government have established several markers that will set the pace of the return to this new normality. These markers will be related to the control of contagions, to the recovery of our health system, and every weekly advance that these markers make possible the opening of new spaces and the lifting of restrictions on the way to that new normality. Every advance against the epidemic will be followed by a step towards the new normality, a new normality that will be full only when the world has an effective vaccine against COVID-19. In any case, ladies and gentlemen, all the steps towards the new normality that will be taken each week will be staggered and, without a doubt, cautious, very cautious, and all will be accompanied by personal and collective hygiene measures and with measures to control and monitor the contagion. The last thing we must allow, ladies and gentlemen, is a slip because it would mean, rather than a setback, a relapse. This coming week will take the first step towards this new normality with the conclusion of the retrievable paid leave that began on March 30. It is envisaged that all workers whose activities were suspended on that date will be reinstated and, of course, that reinstatement will only affect those workers and the suspension - and I would like to stress this: the suspension is maintained - of all the other activities which have been paralysed since 14 March when the state of alarm is decreed. Workers who rejoin next Monday will have to adopt certain strengthened habits of conduct and hygiene to protect themselves and also to protect their co-workers. In any case, throughout this process of transition to a new normality, I would like to repeat that the Government of Spain has always followed and will always follow the mandate of the World Health Organization, that is, to strike a balance between the response to the health crisis, the mitigation of socio-economic effects and the fulfilment of human rights. And we must be aware that the state of alarm that we are asking to extend today carries with it the restriction that is proportionate and necessary in order, after all, to regain those freedoms to which citizens have every right. That is why it is essential not to forget our obligation as public representatives to be exquisite in the protection of legal guarantees; public health, but also the health of our democracy, goes into this. Ladies and gentlemen, the flag that Spain needs is the flag of unity. At this time of crisis, we must be more united than ever because the threats multiply: to the health, economic and social emergency and to the tiredness, logically, physically and psychological of a citizen who has been confined for almost a month, is added a new problem that we are seeing on a daily basis, and is the danger of misinformation. The National Police warned us a few days ago of the emergence of a million and a half accounts on social networks related to COVID-19, whose only function is to spread false news to manipulate public opinion. I repeat, 1 500 000 profiles intended to spread bubbles and fake news. The fact that one lies about a subject as delicate as illness in times of social uncertainty is also a public health problem and, again, is a fundamentally democratic health problem. In this regard, I would like to appeal once again to the collective responsibility of all the political forces that we represent to the whole of popular sovereignty in this House. These are no time for sterile discussions that distract us from the only battle that matters, which is to defeat the virus so that we can begin the economic and social reconstruction of our country as soon as possible. We must concentrate on continuing to implement the measures resulting from the state of alarm, the enlargement of which we are putting to your vote today; measures that continue all the efforts we have already made and which I would like to recapitulate. We have announced the mobilisation of up to EUR 200 billion to protect and secure families, workers, the social economy, the self-employed, businesses in our country, and so far the estimated impact of the main measures approved is EUR 128,288 million, I repeat, EUR 128,288 million, of which more than EUR 4600 million is earmarked for health issues, more than EUR 119 billion for economic measures, more than EUR 3400 million for labour measures and more than EUR 11 billion for social measures. This means, ladies and gentlemen, the greatest mobilization of public resources in our entire democratic history. And to start with health measures, these are what they want - we have shared it on some other occasion in this rostrum - is, first of all, to guarantee the liquidity of the health system to expand the capacity of our system. Secondly, strengthen research, science to find the cure as soon as possible. Thirdly, to strengthen and protect the homes of older people, which have proved to be a critical point in this crisis; our parents, our mothers, our grandparents, are the most threatened and cannot and should not feel the most helpless. To strengthen, fourthly, educational measures to maintain activities through online, distance-based modalities; to avoid, in the end, inequalities in education through the closure of schools and universities. And, fifthly, to increase isolation measures and, consequently, mobility measures to prevent contagion. Secondly, social measures, backed by EUR 11 billion, aim, firstly, to implement and extend the mortgage debt moratorium. Secondly, automatic renewal of rental contracts. Thirdly, articulate microcredits for the payment of up to six months of rent through the ICO for tenants in an unfavourable situation. Fourthly, apply the moratorium on payment to rent for those tenants in vulnerable situations. To prohibit, fifthly, the suspension of basic supplies and ensure food supply. Sixth, extending the electric social bonus to the self-employed and allowing them to suspend electricity and gas bills and pay them for the next six months is a reality at the end of the state of alarm. Seventhly, ensure the minimum transport requirement. Eighthly, we must also ensure the protection of victims of gender-based violence, who are now facing the double threat we have referred to in previous debates, such as the virus itself and forced coexistence with its abusers. Ninthly, monitor consumer prices so that market prices remain fair in materials that are now essential for the general public, and finally, maintain legal attention and prohibit the increase in funeral prices. Thirdly, economic measures are aimed at financing, providing liquidity and also a credit line. In addition, the payment of taxes has been postponed to a maximum of EUR 30 000 for SMEs and self-employed workers for a period of six months, with three months of lack of interest payments. In addition, we have promoted aid to specific productive sectors. As you know, we have speeded up customs clearance and strengthened support for the autonomous communities and also for local authorities. And fourthly, there are the necessary labour measures, which set out the following. Firstly, teleworking as a measure of labour flexibility, understanding and being aware of the difficulties it poses for certain jobs and territories in our country. The implementation, second, of a retrievable paid permit from 30 March to 9 April. It ends, therefore, today. For whom? Of course, as we have said, for workers in non-essential sectors and with the limitations you already know. We have temporarily limited the objective dismissals for causes linked to COVID-19. We have allocated more than EUR 3 million for the special allowance for lack of activity for persons integrated into the Special System for Home Employees of the General Social Security System, and also add up to more than EUR 17 million for the exceptional unemployment allowance for the end of a temporary contract. The calculation of the maximum duration of temporary contracts has also been interrupted. We have automatically extended unemployment benefits. We have allocated more than EUR 230 million for the hiring of 200 000 rural workers in the municipalities, and for small and medium-sized enterprises and self-employed people we have postponed the payment of social security debts for businesses and self-employed persons until 30 June; we have put in place the six-month moratorium on social security contributions and the extraordinary termination benefit for self-employed workers. As regards measures for ERTE, workers entering an ERTE without meeting the unemployment requirements will receive the unemployment benefit, and SMEs with fewer than 50 workers entering this ERTE process will be exempt from paying business contributions. With regard to public employees, the autonomous calendar of public employment offers for teaching bodies, which comprise 27,000 places, is coordinated; public employees are allowed to perform tasks other than those of the workplace and voluntarily support those areas that require reinforcement as a result of COVID-19; contracts for assistants, assistant doctors, associate teachers and visiting teachers are extended, as well as contracts for research staff are extended. Finally, we have promoted special measures for the essential sectors, such as the opening of certain hotel establishments and the access of carriers to certain services and maintenance in service areas. Ladies and gentlemen, I should like to summarise, albeit briefly, the impact of these measures on Spanish society. Firstly, a first line of EUR 20 billion has already been set up from the ICO line for State funding coverage, and I would like to announce to you that in the next extraordinary Council of Ministers to be held this Friday we will launch the second line of EUR 20 billion, of new EUR 20 billion of ICO lines. The Government's estimates suggest that more than 1.3 million self-employed people and businesses could benefit from this, which will be able to meet their obligations and thus ensure the continuity of their activities after the difficulties resulting from this health crisis have ended. In addition, the extension of the ICO's borrowing limit in order to increase the lines of financing to businesses, which are autonomous in order to provide them with the additional liquidity they are demanding, with EUR 10 billion, would in this case benefit another 130 000 businesses and workers and self-employed. From the Government of Spain we want all those activities that are perfectly viable, but that are having transitional difficulties - and I stress the transitional ones - as a result of the health crisis of COVID-19 and the state of alarm can overcome this gap, which is temporary - which is temporary - and can continue its activity. As far as self-employed workers are concerned, more than 588 000 self-employed have already applied for an extra benefit for cessation of activity or for a reduction, as you know, of at least 75% of the turnover compared to the average for the previous six months. According to the Government's forecasts, those who will finally benefit from this measure will reach the million. As for the extra subsidy for lack of activity for domestic workers - a collective representing, as you know, almost 400,000 people, mostly women - they will also benefit from this aid. With regard to the protection measure for workers directly affected by COVID-19, more than 300,000 workers have already been taken in, who are forced to keep quarantine because of the risk of infecting their co-workers. As far as housing is concerned, up to 1,200 000 tenants could benefit from the measures by postponing the payment of rents in the event of economic difficulties arising from the crisis in COVID-19. In the area of agriculture, and in order to ensure the food chain and supply by addressing labour force needs during the collection season, the Government is going to finance the recruitment of 200 000 workers by the municipalities with €236 million, as I said earlier. We will also make it easier to hire temporary workers, allowing for compatibility between income from agricultural work and public benefits to bring in between 75 000 and 80 000 people. In addition, the deadlines for the subscription of agricultural insurance and the refinancing of drought loans are extended by EUR 2.5 million, and also, as some autonomous communities have requested, the deadline for applying for CAP subsidies from this year to 15 May is extended. Finally, with regard to measures to ensure the provision of energy to vulnerable consumers, more than 1 250 000 families could benefit. Among these measures we have also carried out an extension of the electric social bond for six months, which will be able to accommodate more than 420,000 families. Ladies and gentlemen, I would like to emphasise the idea that each and every one of these measures that the Government has taken has a clear and fundamental priority, which is to overcome this emergency as soon as possible by strengthening our public system, especially public health, without leaving anyone behind, especially protecting the most vulnerable and those who suffer from greater insecurity. Ladies and gentlemen, I shall now inform you of the various orders, resolutions and instructions issued by the four ministries designated as competent delegated authorities and published in the BOE since 25 March, when I last appeared in this House. For his part, the Minister of Health has approved a total of fifteen orders, among which we can highlight the one that establishes the conditions for the dispensation and administration of medicines within the scope of the National Health System in order to ensure the supply. The one that determines the removal of the confinement of the municipalities of Igualada, Vilanova del Camí, Santa Margarida de Montbui and Ódena. The one banning the vigils, postpones the holding of funeral ceremonies until the end of the state of alarm and also limits the price rise of funeral services. It authorises the use of bioethanol in gels and hydroalcoholic hand disinfection solutions. The process of obtaining licences for companies engaged in the manufacture, import and grouping or sterilisation of health products is speeded up in order to ensure supply. And the one ordering the Secretariat of State for Digitization and Artificial Intelligence to develop urgently, among other tools to support the management of the health crisis of COVID-19, a computer application that allows the self-assessment of symptoms and the geolocation of the user for the sole purpose of verifying that he is in the autonomous community in which he declares to be. For his part, the Minister of the Interior has issued four orders. One, to extend the controls at the internal land borders, established in the wake of the health crisis. Another, to avoid doubts about the inclusion of vehicles intended for the development of certain essential activities that are not expressly mentioned in previous orders and thus facilitate the work of the State Security Forces and Corpses. Another, to extend the licences and authorizations of weapons whose period of validity expires during the state of alarm and to establish that during the first half of the year the shooting exercises are not carried out for the maintenance of the ability in the conservation, management and use of weapons by security guards. And the fourth, to facilitate registration and change of ownership of certain vehicles. For his part, the Minister of Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda has issued eleven orders and six resolutions. The most relevant are the following. Firstly, the order identifying the tourist accommodation that should be considered essential and should therefore remain open. Orders prohibiting flights with Italy until the end of the state of alarm and therefore extending the ban on the entry of passenger ships from the Italian Republic and cruise ships of any origin. The order regulating a second acquisition and distribution of masks for subsequent distribution to personnel who provide their services in the transport sector in areas of risk. The order allowing to reduce the offer of passenger transport services, on the occasion of the entry into force of the retrievable paid permit. And the two resolutions that come from the Directorate General of Civil Aviation to guarantee air transport service on certain routes of the Canary Islands and Palma de Mallorca and Ibiza. Finally, the Minister of Defence has issued an order to suspend, temporarily and exceptionally, any regulatory restrictions on the distance teaching of subjects in the different curricula. Ladies and gentlemen, the action of the Government does not, of course, end at our borders. One of the major challenges facing us in this pandemic has to do with Europe, with the European Union. They know what the Spanish Government has always advocated in relation to Europe and the demand for solidarity it entails. The extraordinary of this emergency requires strength, new measures. Otherwise - and I say, moreover, without nuances - our union is in danger. We need unbridled solidarity and a solid proof that the cohesion that defines us as a Community project is more than mere rhetoric. As President of the Government of Spain, of a firmly European government - because Spanish society is firmly European - that is what I demand of the European Union. And we don't have time to waste in this either; it's now or never. We already have on the table the European Central Bank's temporary emergency purchase programme, unpublished and applauded. We also have a design for an unemployment reinsurance fund, the so-called SURE Plan. Let's see what the details of that plan are, but we need a lot more; it's not enough. The reconstruction of Europe as a union of countries is as important as the unity of action in our own country. We therefore need to be demanding, ladies and gentlemen, we need measures to support the public indebtedness of many states, because this crisis is not guilty, it is a global emergency and therefore a European emergency. Europe has to confront COVID as expected, it has to act now to deal with the disease, and it will have to act later by mobilizing all the resources that are needed to implement a new Marshall Plan. We must learn the lesson, austerity after the 2008 crisis has only brought fragility to the disaster. Neither austerity nor cuts are the way. We do not want fragility; on the contrary, we want strength, action, unity and freedom for all. In short, we want reconstruction and that reconstruction should be initiated so that it has been more fragile since the time of austerity in 2008, which is the public, our state of well-being. Financial mechanisms that cover the reconstruction and repair of countries following this crisis should not be governed by inequality and punishment in the face of the so-called smaller economies. Today we are all fighting - and I stress it, all - in the face of the same emergency, an unprecedented emergency. Tomorrow, all the countries will have to rise with the same spirit and support from Europe, the twenty-seven countries. It is now that Europe must be more Europe than ever. It is now that we must do so together because, otherwise, the Union will have forgotten the Europeans and, logically, the Spanish. Ladies and gentlemen, I also ask you to contribute to this task. All of you have a voice in Europe through your respective brotherly parties and their respective European parliamentary groups; I humbly ask you to make it heard, to make yourselves heard. I invite you to defend Spain and Europe. I urge you to show your co-religionaries that this pandemic has no distinction between the north and the south, as it does not distinguish between left or right governments either. It is the most ferocious catastrophe humanity has endured in three quarters of a century and Europe cannot look elsewhere. The Spanish parties are European parties and these European parties have a voice and influence in national governments and also in the European Parliament. I therefore ask you to act in favour of Spain and the European Union. This is the time for unity of action, also by this House, at European level. [[Applauds]] Ladies and gentlemen, I know that we are living in hard times of confinement that are undermining our mood, I understand the fatigue of confinement, especially that of those families with the worst conditions of habitability. I understand the concern for the future, especially that of our compatriots in situations of greater vulnerability. But I want to convey two ideas that I think are fundamental at this very critical moment. The first is that no one will be abandoned to his fate. The collective effort will ensure that the wake of civilian casualties is not followed by a mass of social victims. And the second is that only resistance, social discipline and science will allow us to overcome this very hard trance. As you know, ladies and gentlemen, COVID-19 has opened a threefold crisis around the world, we have said on many occasions - all of you repeatedly say in your various public appearances - a health crisis, an economic crisis and a social crisis. From Germany to China, from the United States to France, from Italy to Japan or Great Britain, every day that all nations pass is aware that it was not an isolated fact of one or two nations, but rather, as WHO has said, a global pandemic. This is therefore a global emergency. But, ladies and gentlemen, only in Spain, at least among the big countries, has the virus opened up a fourth crisis, which is the political crisis. I'm not saying it, it's seen as prestigious headlines, such as the Financial Times, over the last week. In a recent article, this economic journal was surprised that, while in all countries the pandemic has served to minimize political rivalries, in Spain it is serving to confront the political confrontation. This week, for example, we listened to the conservative leader of Portugal, in the opposition, making an emotional speech wishing him luck and good luck to the Prime Minister, António Costa, because his luck and his right - the leader of the opposition said - would be those of all Portugal. [[Applauds]] We were also listening to the newly elected British Labour leader, unqualifiedly standing beside Boris Johnson's conservative British government, whom I take advantage of, by the way, to wish him a speedy and complete cure. Ladies and gentlemen, while our health care providers are fighting in the front line, while our agents and military personnel are fully employed, while our farmers and staff dependent on small and large areas are feeding us, while our families are isolated to protect themselves, I believe that we must be united, as they and they are. No one understands that this is not the case. We are, before we, the representatives of all the Spaniards, think what they think; therefore I humbly ask you for unity and loyalty. Notice, the Regional Director of Europe of the World Health Organization, Hans Kluge, concluded last week the mission commissioned by that organization to analyse the situation in our country in terms of response to COVID-19. So that there is no room for interpretation, I quote your words textually after the end of that mission. He said: I am deeply impressed with the heroism of the professionals who are on the front line, with the solidarity of the Spanish people as a whole and with the inspiring resolve of the Government. The WHO Regional Director in Europe continued: I feel cautious optimism as a result of the bold measures, innovative approaches and courageous decisions of Minister of Health Salvador Illa. The Director-General of the World Health Organization reiterated those words. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, I do not ask the opposition to subscribe to these words, I only humbly ask for unity and loyalty. It is clear that the world, that Europe, that all the West has arrived late and that Spain is no exception, but if Spain stands out in anything it would be for four reasons that I would like to share with all of you. First, Spain is the country that first took measures of confinement throughout the West, the first. We did it with only 120 dead, far ahead of the other countries. Secondly, Spain is one of the countries that is performing - and growing - an average of 20,000 tests per inhabitant per day. Thirdly, Spain is the country of the West that has gone further in the containment measures, because what we announce, ladies and gentlemen, we execute. And finally, Spain is the country that provides more information, the only country that reports all the positives, not only those hospitalized; all the positives, all the deceased diagnosed, all the income in the ICU and all the highs. All of Europe, ladies and gentlemen, was late but Spain acted before. I do not ask you to sign it, the only thing I ask of the opposition is unity and loyalty. [[Applauds]] Of course, ladies and gentlemen, these data come from sources as undisputed as the European political environment or the COVID-19 study of a prestigious British university, the University of Oxford. Precisely, this study, that of the University of Oxford, gives Spain the highest score of Western countries, ninety out of a hundred; ninety out of a hundred in terms of rigour in the response to the pandemic. I am sure that for the opposition 100 over 100 it would have been better than 90 over 100, as well as for the Government; there are things that we would have done differently with today's knowledge yesterday, but yesterday we acted with yesterday's knowledge not with today's knowledge. No Government in any country can presume that it has not made any mistake, ladies and gentlemen, none. We are fighting against an enemy, the virus, which is unknown to science and, consequently, to experts who advise the Government and also to Governments. The self-requirement of the Government of Spain, ladies and gentlemen, is absolute. What I can assure you is that the Spanish Government has been working and has always done so in good faith. He has worked with all his energy, with all his means, with the advice of the best experts our country has. And I am also convinced, ladies and gentlemen, that the governments of all the autonomous communities, of all the municipalities - without distinction of political colour - are acting with the same will. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, I do not demand recognition; all I demand of your ladies and gentlemen is unity and loyalty. The Director General of the OECD, Ángel Gurría, was this very week very clear about the pandemic, the economy and also about our country. He said the following: We are faced with an unprecedented situation and we must take unprecedented measures. It is a false dilemma if one chooses between economics or health, at the moment the only thing that can be done - said the Mexican Angel Gurría - is to fight the pandemic with all the elements that you have. First, care must be taken for the unemployed, who have been left out of work. In the case of Spain - said the Secretary-General of the OECD - what they are doing now is very exemplary from the point of view of confinement and that will allow them to end faster with the spread of the virus. The pandemic will end - said the Mexican Angel Gurría - especially if they apply, as in Spain, measures as important as those we are applying. Ladies and gentlemen, I do not ask for such statements by the opposition, all I ask is unity and loyalty. Ladies and gentlemen, our country is experiencing extreme times and the health crisis that we are only beginning to bring back is accompanied by a great economic and social crisis that we are already beginning to feel. The whole of Europe, the whole of the West, wonders whether it has reacted late to the health crisis. Time will have to understand what needs to be improved and what needs to be avoided so that a pandemic of these characteristics does not happen again, but what we cannot afford is to be late for the economic and social crisis that we already have. We need to react soon and we need to do it together, all of us. In that regard, I made the proposal for new Moncloa pacts, which I formulated last Saturday and which I would like to reiterate here today and now solemnly before this House. If our health workers fight the virus together; if our police, our military, face the virus; if our farmers, our dependents - as I said before - remain in their positions against the virus; if our families are held together to protect themselves from the virus; if the destruction of employment affects us all; if the crisis puts the well-being of all; if it strikes everyone without distinguishing political preferences, what does division mean?, who benefits the division? We have a precedent in our democratic history on which we can draw inspiration. Our country has already faced a different, but also dramatic situation, which threatened to take our economy ahead and, with it, our newly reconquered democracy, and the Spaniards were able to respond together, without distinction of creeds or ideologies. We identified a danger and planted face together with those Pacts of the Moncloa. That same formula is what we are proposing, with the logical modifications that correspond to the transformation that our State has undergone, today a strongly decentralized State. Therefore, what I propose is a great agreement for the economic and social reconstruction of Spain involving all the forces willing to hold their shoulders: political parties of course, but also entrepreneurs, trade unions and, of course, the autonomous communities, the provincial parliaments and the municipalities. I propose an agreement for the economic and social reconstruction of Spain. In recent days, I have heard some reactions that express reservations about this initiative. It has been said that it cannot involve a change of regime; well, 100 per cent agree, the regime is and must remain our social and democratic Constitution, which has full force and must inspire us to its full extent. It has been said that it cannot nullify the parliamentary control of the Government; fully agree, full force of parliamentary control. It has been said that there can be no reduction in information transparency; absolute compliance must remain absolute information transparency, as we have been doing since the beginning of this crisis. It has been said that it cannot exclude anyone; perfect, all those who are willing to tie their shoulders - as I said before - are invited and want to do so. It has been said that you are suspicious of the sincerity of the proposal; well, I repeat the proposal from the heart and also formally express it in this House, with all solemnity, to the legitimate representatives of all the Spaniards, who are you. Ladies and gentlemen, in this crisis, which has unfortunately already been extended to a great many citizens, I have had to address our fellow citizens as responsible for the Government of Spain on several occasions, and every time I have done so I have repeated the same words: sacrifice, resistance and the moral of victory. And the Spaniards are demonstrating, beyond the political color and government preference they have, that they know how to respond with sacrifice, with resistance and with a moral of victory. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, I formally call you next week for a first meeting of all political forces who want to participate in this great economic and social agreement for the reconstruction of our country. We will include in this agreement the social partners, the autonomous presidents and the political forces that are willing to hold their shoulders. We're all called to those pacts. Today, in this House which brings together legitimate representatives, I ask you only what I have been repeating during this last part of my speech: unity and loyalty, which is the way in which political leaders can contribute to the sacrifice, resistance and morale of victory that the whole of our people is demonstrating. And I end with a question, ladies and gentlemen, today, here and now: are you ready? I am. I only ask you what I can offer you: unity and loyalty. Thank you. [[Applauds]]

Thirdly, add a new point to Article 3, which is: given what is happening with the sanitary material and what some desalinated people are planning to do, that the Government intervenes in setting prices for the sanitary material such as masks, gloves, disinfectant gels, etc. Fourthly, at such a dramatic moment in which we have a financing system that generates great inequalities, we understand that it is absolutely necessary for every citizen to receive the same per capita financing in order to be able to deal with this crisis with the same weapons; we therefore call for discretionary transfers to the autonomous communities so that none is below the average; in addition, that the liquidity instruments for the autonomous communities, the deliveries on account of all the financing planned for the year 2020, the loan on the settlement of the value added tax of 2017 or the funds of autonomous liquidity, the normal and the extra, and the suspension of the payment of the interests of the FLA. Fifth - I think this is a proposal that can be implemented now -, that the Administration can continue to bid telematically so that it does not now contribute to curbing the economy, so that when we go out we can start the works already; I give an example: a public school, if we can continue to bid now, if we can continue to work on it, when we go out we can start the works already, and we already know what that means: we would advance the economic recovery. I know that these measures will be taken together and that they may not be adopted, but I ask you to pick them up in that new decree that you will take out on Saturday. Finally, a plea and a proposal. Mr President, ministers, ministers, watch the banks, watch the banks. Hundreds - hundreds - of complaints from the autonomous are coming to us. And finally, a very constitutionalist proposal, as your right wing gentlemen likes. Let's be real constitutionalists. I'm done. Let us be constitutionalists of the rights of people, non-constitutionalists of bracelet and mask with the flag. Why isn't there...? Don't you dare? ParlaMint-ES_2020-04-09-CD200409.u42	Thank you very much, ladies and gentlemen. Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, I would like to thank all the parliamentary groups, from the most constructive point of view, for their support for this decree extending the state of alarm. The first thing I would like to say to you is that, without a doubt, there will be time to examine in detail all the lessons of this crisis; to analyse the paradigm shift that it is going to assume, which is already assuming in political action, in decision-making and in its relation to public policies, and to see what can be improved. Here various parliamentary groups have made their reflections and contributions on what issues we need to improve in the field of public coverage, in the area of strengthening or not the welfare state and, without a doubt, in the construction of Europe. The whole legislature is left to do so, ladies and gentlemen, so we can discuss this debate when it comes to it. But before expressing these considerations, there are some questions that we should all answer here and now without doubt, as representatives of the Spaniards we are, especially the ladies and gentlemen who have been most skeptical in their speeches regarding this agreement for the economic and social reconstruction of our country which is proposing the Government of Spain, a sort of new pacts of the Moncloa or as you logically wish to describe them. Ladies and gentlemen, the question is very simple: What is better? Is it better to have an emergency military unit or not? Is it better to have a robust or weak health system? Is it better to have an education or science and research system, which is now essential to fight the pandemic, robust or weak? What's better, having a powerful fiber network that supports our communications at such extreme times where it is clearly necessary to strengthen digitisation over the next few years or not to have it? What's better, having well-trained and trained security forces or not? What is better, having a care system, in our senior residences, for the elderly or not? What's better, seventeen autonomous communities fighting with each other to see who is buying that sanitary material in a market absolutely tense as a result of a global pandemic or seventeen autonomous communities rowing in the same direction? What's better, science or whim, ladies and gentlemen? What's better, unity or division? That's the question. In some speeches I appreciate, logically, as president, that they are afraid of the agreement, because they believe that this demonstrates a supposed weakness, when it is just the opposite; that they would show strength before all the Spaniards, they would show greatness. It is the Government that makes an offer of concertation, and with this they would show not only an exercise of maturity, but something much more important to promote that unity of action to which we are appealing, which is humility, is closeness, is transparency. Only those who do not believe in politics as an instrument for agreement give up an agreement before attempting it. Agreements are often reached as dialogue begins with a constructive attitude, even when it is believed that the fruits they are going to bear are small or are called into question. Why, therefore, is dialogue renounced? There is no answer to this question when it is precisely in a sincere way, as the Government is doing, and also in the face of an unprecedented national emergency, but not only in Spain, in the world, ladies and gentlemen. So I would like to ask you again, therefore, a very simple question which you have to answer here and which you cannot avoid. Some have done so, have responded in a direct, clear, and blunt way. We already know, I imagined, but what is better, unity or division for those who have a sense of state or at least claim to have a sense of state? I believe, ladies and gentlemen, that our democracy is rocky because it defends individual rights at the same time as it defends the common good and because it allows there to be, as there have been during these forty-s many years of democracy, conservative governments and socialist governments, and today, for the first time in the history of democracy, a coalition government. To parties that have taken a constructive position, even if it is critical, I would no doubt like to thank them for their support for the uncertainty from which transcendental decisions are made. Of course, those political parties that have the honour of leading the autonomous communities in circumstances such as the present are clearly obliged to make decisions not in hours, in minutes, ladies and gentlemen, and that does not mean that we do not have the vocation of dialogue or concertation, but that we logically need to respond in a matter of minutes. And to the parties that have taken another position, I want to tell you what I have been saying over these last few days, these last few weeks, and that we do not see them as adversaries and that the Government that I have the honour to preside over, which is a coalition government, is going to continue to reach out to all the parliamentary forces that want to hold their shoulders. Because that is where we are, ladies and gentlemen, pulling our shoulders to save lives and then rebuilding our country and Europe socially and economically. And from Spain we can also contribute to that social, economic reconstruction and I would say that even morale of our continent, of our common project. I believe that the pandemic is proving at a global level that a country alone cannot overcome it, it cannot, and within our country we will not be able to overcome it if there is no unity around the Government, a unity that does not have to be acrylic but, of course, critical, constructive, loyal. I said this at the beginning of my speech: I only offer what I can ask of you, which is unity and loyalty. We need the whole of Spanish society - the whole of Spanish society - to live where they live, to represent what they represent in the social order that we have, be economic actors, be social actors, be political forces, be governments of different colors at the autonomous level, at the provincial level and at the municipal level. That is why, throughout my speech, I have asked you only what I can offer, which is unity and loyalty, and at this point I would like to clarify some issues and be precise on several points because this will logically be recorded in the minutes of the session of the Congress of Deputies. That is why I want to be rigorous in some of the issues that have been raised here, and that is why I want to read in this reply some of the issues that have been raised here, so that they are recorded, because we know that this debate is going to come to an end in this pandemic. In recent weeks, ladies and gentlemen, some information and statements from some political leaders have focused on managing the response deployed by the Government of Spain with the aim of reducing the impact, doubling the pandemic and eradicating the disease. These statements have tried to draw a hugely negative picture, as we have seen from some political groups on the opposition bench; not in all, in some - predictable, logically. Today we have heard some of them repeated in this House. These are statements based on palpable falsehoods; palpable falsehoods, ladies and gentlemen, and I want it to be recorded in the record. They have been repeated to the point of satiety, such as trying to keep the noise from hearing the signal, among other things, some of the following statements: first, that the Spanish Government rejected in the first weeks of the expansion of the virus the calls made by the World Health Organization and the European Union to collect sanitary material during the first few weeks of the expansion of COVID-19. That has been said by the conservative bench. Secondly, it was late in international markets, creating a bottleneck that prevented the autonomous communities themselves from providing health-care resources. This has also been said by the conservative bench. That the size of the number of infected in Spain is not known because very few diagnoses are made regarding other European countries. This has also been said by the conservative bench. Ladies and gentlemen, if we really wanted to stick to objective and contrasting data, it would serve to disassemble this thesis from the very recent report prepared by the World Health Organization on the measures implemented in our country to combat the pandemic. This report was prepared by the Special Envoy, who is working closely with the Government of Spain, whose mission ended two days ago. That report, ladies and gentlemen, has resulted in some public statements by several members of the WHO - which I will remind you later but which I also anticipated in my first speech - on, I insist, the heroism - I will never tire of saying this - of the public workers who are on the front line, the solidarity of the people and the inspiring resolution of the Minister and the Ministry of Health. Obviously, we must recognise - and this has already been said by the Government - that, together with the appropriate measures, better measures can also be identified today. I have said this, ladies and gentlemen, and I do not know why it is being said that the Government of Spain does not take self-criticism; I am saying that logically, with the knowledge we have today, some things from yesterday would make them different. But today's knowledge is not the knowledge of yesterday, not only for the governments - be it Spanish, Autonomous Government X or European Government Y - but also for science, ladies and gentlemen, which is facing a virus that does not know how to treat. We don't have a vaccine, we don't have a therapeutic clarity about how to fight the virus. Therefore, a Government must always be self-demanding. I said this in my first speech, ladies and gentlemen: this government is absolutely self-demanding, self-critical and humble. We hear all the contributions, ladies and gentlemen, all the contributions, not only those of the parliamentary forces, but also those of the autonomous governments. Since the beginning of the crisis not only are there conferences of presidents every week, but also all the authorities delegated to this alarm decree have been meeting through videoconferences, regularly, weekly - up to two or three times, as the Ministry of Health does, with health advisers -. With some of them, in places where there is a greater focus or intensity of the pandemic, the conversations are even daily, as happens for example with the health adviser of the Community of Madrid. Therefore, a government must always be self-demanding and, as I have repeatedly said in all my speeches, the right time will come, ladies and gentlemen, to hold a debate on all of this and to address it in an area that I raised in my first intervention with the Alarm Decree, I refer to the parliamentary committee of study and evaluation to know exactly what has happened and how we can use better public instruments to respond to possible future pandemics. Well, on coordination with the World Health Organization, the European Union and the autonomous communities, some clear ideas for some to be left in the retina, those who continue to insist on lying and bulls. Some clear ideas: Spain has always acted, from the very beginning, in coordination with the WHO and the European Union. In fact, when the WHO declared the global health emergency on 30 January, with 7700 cases worldwide and none in Spain, our country already complied with all the recommendations. On 3 February, with 17,381 cases and one case in Spain, WHO published a draft of the Plan for Strategic Preparation for the New Coronavirus - a framework document containing measures for national coordination, communication to citizens, identification and treatment of cases, logistics and provision of essential services for different scenarios - and in this evaluation, carried out by WHO on 30 March, Spain was at the highest level of response capacity. I'm not saying that, says the WHO. On 4 February, with 27,597 cases in the world and one in Spain, an interterritorial council was convened. All the autonomous communities appreciated the coordination and information they received daily from the ministry and it was found that the protocols were working, from a government - I insist, ladies and gentlemen - that takes possession at the beginning of January, in the second week of January. On 13 February, ladies and gentlemen, with 60 117 cases in the world and 2 in Spain, the EPSCO health group met in Brussels - formation of the European Council bringing together the Ministers for Employment, Social Policy, Health and Consumers - and on that occasion the European Commission warned of the risk and urged countries to prepare for a global epidemic. The Minister of Health reported that preventive and preparatory measures were already being implemented in Spain fully in line with WHO, with ECDC, which is the scientific body at European level, and other coordination mechanisms. At the Interterritorial Council of 25 February last, with 80 250 cases in the world and 2 in Spain, the protocols were validated by agreement of all the autonomous communities and the different scenarios were proposed to give a common response in case the risk profile increased. At the Interterritorial Council on 1 March, with 88,913 cases worldwide and 140 in Spain, the autonomous communities were asked to update their contingency plans. On 6 March, with 96 000 global cases - 374 cases in Spain and 5 deaths -, in the second extraordinary EPSCO, the Minister of Health stressed that at that time measures were particularly necessary to ensure the availability of resources through joint purchasing procedures at European level. On that date, ladies and gentlemen, the European Union launched a joint tender for glasses, masks, breathers, diagnostic kits, etc. Spain participated in all joint purchasing proposals. On 12 March, with 124 519 global cases - in Spain, 2950 cases and 84 deaths - the ECDC proposed, depending on the increased risk to Europe at that time, social distancing measures. Spain had already implemented them in areas of significant transmission - for example, in the Autonomous Community of Madrid - and that same day they were extended to all the autonomous communities of our country. On 14 March, with 142,320 global cases, in Spain, with 5753 recognized cases of contagion and 136 deaths, the state of alarm was declared. The Government was empowered to establish a centralized supply when any medicinal product or health protection product was depleted. This means, ladies and gentlemen, that, in view of the scarcity of the area and in order to guarantee health care throughout the territory, national purchases and production could be ordered and redistributed with equity and cohesion in the face of this pandemic that was affecting the whole country without distinction of territories. Ladies and gentlemen, the Autonomous Communities have maintained their competences in health care. There hasn't been a recentralization exercise here. Let us be serious and rigorous at least in this appreciation. We haven't re-centralized anything. Communities have maintained their health skills and have been able to purchase materials at all times. In no case has the competence of the Autonomous Communities been changed, which continues to be responsible for managing and providing health care and providing the technical and human resources necessary to meet these obligations. Autonomous communities, for example, Galicia, Euskadi, Valencian Community, Catalonia, Navarre or Castile-La Mancha - i.e. communities of different signs, and I mean in terms of autonomous governments: left, right, nationalists or non-nationalists - have carried out these purchasing operations overcoming enormous difficulties. Others, such as the Community of Madrid, took weeks to try and took refuge behind apologies such as the bottleneck. No material was ever requested, ladies and gentlemen, nor was any import blocked. This is demonstrated by the arrival of aircraft ten days after the announcement. I shall now refer to the arrival of supply, ladies and gentlemen. With regard to the arrival of personal protective items and sanitary appliances, an average daily aircraft with sanitary materials has been on land in Spain for more than two weeks and is being distributed immediately to all the autonomous communities. This weekend four million masks have been received, in addition to other pieces, such as glasses, gloves and protective coats. These are some figures as of April 5, I want to remind you of them. With regard to the elements of individual protection, it has been said that the Government started buying after the alarm decree on 14 March. This statement is false, because before that date more than 414,000 masks had already been distributed - and I want to say it here for the record - to the whole territory and other protective equipment. Since 10 March, that is, long before, four days before the state of alarm was declared, the Spanish Government was already doing so. Prior to that date, between 14 March and 21 March, an additional 3.7 million masks were distributed to the autonomous communities, among other equipment and health products, through purchase processes and ongoing donations. Insufficient? Certainly insufficient, ladies and gentlemen, but it must be acknowledged that this Government has made an unequivocal effort to provide and equip all the autonomous communities with the necessary PPE in order to cope with this pandemic, as we are facing. From the beginning of March until 5 April, the Spanish Government has transferred to the health authorities of the various territories a total of 43 million units, ladies and gentlemen, 43 million units, including almost 25 million masks for patients and professionals, 18 million gloves, 155 000 protective glasses, 238 000 disposable coats and divers and more than 500 assisted breathing equipment, which are fundamental to our ICUs. The Government has signed contracts totalling EUR 850 million, which will continue to arrive from abroad in the coming days. But in addition, and in a strategic plan - for those who have raised it in their interventions - to seek self-sufficiency - because I believe that this is also one of the lessons we have to draw from this crisis - agreements have been reached to produce much of this equipment in Spain. I was just a few days ago in a factory in Móstoles, which is a second generation family company, which is now passing from producing fifty respirators a day to producing more than one hundred respirators a day, in addition to large automotive corporations that, in fact, thanks to the impulse of new technologies are producing respirators that can somehow alleviate the deficit we have in our ICU beds. We have the example of the case of the contract with the Mondragon group, which has been promoted by the Government of Spain, which is going to manufacture 60 million masks in the next six months at a rate of 10 million masks per month to ensure in that descalation that we are so concerned about everyone, ladies and gentlemen, the necessary provision of masks of protective material, not only for the workers but also to recover our new normality, to which I referred earlier in my intervention. About the respirators, I said before - last week I was in a factory visiting the workers and entrepreneurs of that company - the government has bought more than 1500 respirators in a complicated international market and those who rule in autonomous communities know very well that there is enormous tension in the market. We have received more than 2000 donations, which we appreciate, and we have successfully promoted two national manufacturing projects that were confirmed and communicated last Tuesday, as I said before, in relation to Seat and also with a company in Móstoles called Hersill. I therefore believe that we can ensure that maximum levels can be reached to provide all intensive care units with invasive mechanical ventilation equipment throughout the territory. I shall now refer to the rapid tests and the PCRs, which have been referred to by some of you in your speeches. To extend the analyses on the effects of the Spanish pandemic, the Government has implemented a strategy based on two complementary actions - and this I want it to be recorded because there is also a lot of misinformation in some of the interventions that the right bank has made from that forum -: increase the PCR, first of all, the diagnostic tests that are the most reliable and that are being carried out from the beginning of the crisis in hospitals. They are used in Korea in China and Spain, which is one of the countries, by the way, with the highest number of tests performed. I say this and I support it, ladies and gentlemen, we are one of the countries with the highest number of tests carried out, around 20 000 daily, 20 000 daily RCPs. Secondly, it complements the rapid diagnostic tests, once determined by the National Microbiology Centre, which have a reliability of between 64 and 80 %. Positive results will count as cases of contagion and negative ones, in the case of suspected patients, will be contrasted with a PCR test. In other words, these last few hours are being transferred to the autonomous communities one million rapid tests and another four million rapid tests have been contracted, as I communicated to the autonomous communities at the last Conference of Presidents held last Sunday by videoconference. The priority areas for carrying them out logically must be the hospitals and the residences of the elderly. But we have also focused on other initiatives, to which I also referred in my first speech. For example, senior residences, which are the competence of the autonomous communities. We have always shown our most absolute willingness to help. The residences of the elderly, ladies and gentlemen, are, as you know, the competence of the autonomous communities. However, the Government of Spain authorized the action of the Armed Forces for disinfection, issued several orders through the Ministry of Health, even before the decree of the state of alarm, with protocols to separate the positive cases from the rest, and even empowered the autonomous health authorities to intervene in these establishments for reasons of health of residents. The last initiative is to demand detailed information on the actual status of each of them in order to take decisions that are deemed appropriate. It is considered as a matter of utmost priority for health authorities to provide residences with more analytical tests to identify and treat the number of contagions and other protective elements. In recent days we have also seen that the number of new cases, and especially new ones hospitalized and admitted to ICU, is slowing down. I believe that in this we must all be unanimous, at least in the assessment of the figures. That we'd like her to be older? Undoubtedly, but here are the effects of the state of alarm and the so drastic measures that we are taking and so harsh for all the homes of our country. The sacrifice of so many millions of Spaniards and the titanic task of health professionals are allowing the curve of new contagions and new hospitalizations to stabilize and even descend in some areas; there are some areas of our country where it has already descended. That's because we all, or almost all, row in the same direction. Experts say you're walking on the right path. Critics, in my opinion, are always necessary to improve, I mean it, with humility, are welcome, although many of them do not have a constructive intention, as has been made clear in this rostrum. But the misinformation, the practice that practices it, will always be defeated, as today, with dates, figures and data. It is surprising, of course, that these voices come from people who want to make a lot of noise but who make deaf ears to the evidence. It is curious that they continue to boldly repeat that the Government has rejected the appeals of WHO, especially when the WHO authorities have clearly and on several occasions - not on one, but on several occasions - endorsed the opportunity and feasibility of Spanish decisions. Let's do a little review, ladies and gentlemen. The Director-General of WHO, 11 March: Spain is an example of any Government's approach to combating COVID-19. The Director of Public Health of the WHO, the Spanish María Neira, on March 15: All the measures that I have seen decided in Spain have seemed to me very adapted to the situation at every moment. If they have now decided to climb this way, that is, one day after the state of alarm - so important will be because they have assessed that the situation has changed and that it deserves this kind of action. WHO Director for Europe, 5 April: Deeply impressed by the heroism of front-line workers in Spain, the solidarity of people and the inspiring resolution of the Government of Spain. Moderate optimism at bold, innovative measures and courageous decisions. WHO Director General, April 6th: I couldn't agree more, I am very moved by the enormous efforts being made by the Government of Spain and the people to combat COVID-19. WHO is in solidarity with Spain and will continue to provide the necessary support. My thanks go to WHO Europe, on a mission to Spain, led by Dr Bruce Aylward. Among other issues, because right now what is happening in Spain is not that there is a contagion outside the homes, but that there is a contagion within the homes. Containment therefore acts on an obvious reality that we have to respond to, which is the contagion and spread of the pandemic in a very accused way outside our homes, but now, of course, where most of that contagion is occurring is within our homes, for many reasons that have a lot to do with the compact cities that we have in our country, unlike other societies, such as the American, which, except in certain states, have, logically, a type of urbanization, urbanism and coexistence very different from ours. What I want to say with this, Your Honor, is that confinement has to be accompanied by other issues, other measures, which have to do, for example, with the elaboration and carrying out of tests, of the RCPs and, to a lesser extent and in a complementary way - and it has to be supplemented - by rapid tests; it has to be accompanied also by a seroprevalence survey, which the Government of Spain is going to develop over the next few days, and it has to be accompanied by a supply, a collection, a provision - to workers in the first place and, secondly, to citizens as a whole - of the materials necessary to be able to defend themselves and protect themselves and protect themselves from the extension of COVID-19 when we can all go out and recover that new normality.
