Obviously, we have an internal job to do, of debate, also to see the evolution of the pandemic, but that does not mean that we do not have the vocation to dialogue and to agree with other necessary political forces and that they are also demonstrating, like their own, loyalty to the Government of Spain. On the European Union, I agree with you, it is time. I understand the issue of the confidence that you have raised, that we have to give all governments to Europe. This was a government that projected a government deficit reduction horizon at the end of the legislature to 1% or less than 1% of GDP and we were on a path to reducing public debt. All of this, Your Honor, has jumped into the air, but not only for Spain, but for the whole of the European Union, because the immediate economic horizon we have is black. Therefore, we will have to act with all the necessary springs. Mr Matute of Bildu criticises - it is a constructive criticism - that we come every fifteen days to extend the alarm decree. I am already telling you that I am convinced that within 15 days I will have to renew the alarm decree, but if I ask for an alarm decree of one month the opposition says that I want to jump into Parliament, so I prefer to come to this House every 15 days to ask for an extension. I am convinced that within 15 days I will have to do it again, because we will obviously not have put an end to the pandemic and, if I do not ask, it will be because we will have such extraordinary news that I prefer not to say it. Mr Matute, I think I have responded to some of the questions that you and other parliamentary groups have raised about the toughness of the confinement. He believes that for us, Mr Matute, the key is to save lives and defend the health of workers, but I insist that it is very important to be aware that the pandemic is not going to defeat it alone and exclusively with confinement. We have to do other things. The level of confinement we're asking citizens for this long month that we've been in is very hard. Mobility has been drastically reduced and there is no doubt that the state of alarm gives us the capacity to react quickly if we see any resounding, which we hope will not happen in the coming weeks, but I would like to say one thing, Mr Matute, and also to the other groups of Members who have raised this question. We referred to the evil so-called hibernation after Royal Decree-Law 10/2020, but the effects of that hibernation, of that retrievable paid leave, have not yet been seen. What we are recording right now are the results of the containment measures implemented with the first decree and then with its extension. From this weekend on, we will see the effects of these extraordinary measures that we launched almost ten days ago. I say this more than anything because on many occasions we have to be aware of the temporal horizon of the measures we are putting in place. What is very clear is that with the measures we adopted in the first real alarm decree we have stopped - and a lot - mobility because the Spanish have complied with the confinement and restriction of mobility, and that 4% that we are recording right now, which is still high and that we have to reduce to zero, is a consequence of all the efforts we have made since the state of alarm decreed on 14 March. The results of the measures we adopted just over ten days ago will begin to be seen next week. I say this so that we are aware that this data does not have to do directly with economic hibernation, with that retrievable paid permission, but with the alarm decree in its initial design, which is the one that we resume from Monday. This is also important for the analysis and constructive criticism that you logically make and that we assume. I am simply saying this so that I can be more clear on this issue. And I share with you, as other parliamentary groups have done, the issue of self-sufficiency. It is true that we have to guarantee national manufacture. I insist that there is no re-centralisation of policies. It's not in my mind to refocus politics. I am a strong defender of the autonomous state and I am a strong advocate of decentralization of policies. I have always argued that education and health, as Mr Rufián mentioned earlier in his speech, improves in the hands of the administrations that are closest to the citizens. I am a strong supporter of the principle of subsidiarity which governs the European Union. Therefore, we act when we are forced to do so to ensure public health and save lives in each and every territory of our country. Mrs Vehi, spokesman for the UPC, said that we did not defend the common good. Aren't we defending public health? Aren't we supplying it? Insufficiently or late, you will be able to criticize me for that, but this government has regained the universality of public health, has put more than EUR 1 billion in the hands of public health, in the hands of the Minister of Health, precisely to give an equitable and efficient response to this global crisis. Finally, I believe that I can be criticized for something else and I accept the criticism, but saying that we do not defend public health and that we do not defend the welfare state, I think it is a criticism that is not very sympathetic to reality. We're in a paradigm shift, I agree with you. I do not, of course, agree with the solutions that it proposes. And among them is the minimum living income, which is a new public policy that we will put in place as soon as we have the opportunity. Mrs Oramas spoke about the exit from the crisis. I agree with the change in paradigm, in the lessons of the past; there is public health, there are care policies, also of elderly residences - you have ruled in the Canary Islands for many years and you know perfectly well how important it is to strengthen these care policies - and logically we not only defend national cohesion, but also territorial cohesion, and of course we will always try to provide solutions to the Canary Islands. Mr García, from UPN, I only agree with one thing you have said, and that is that Spain is going to move forward, that is for sure. I wish it was with you too, pulling your shoulder. Of course we empathize. I believe that in your intervention what you have come to say indirectly is that we must empathize, and we empathize, we provide solutions. I would like you in your interventions to provide solutions and not just complain; provide solutions, tie your shoulder and we will certainly assume constructive criticism in the most humble way possible. I insist, I offer what I can give, which is unity and loyalty, solutions and certainties, of course we do, Mr García, but, I insist, we have to anticipate scenarios. I wish those scenarios didn't happen, but we have to anticipate them. It's duty. His party has ruled in Navarre for a long time and is also aware that governing, managing, implies anticipating, pedagogy and, therefore, that's what we do, we are not incorporating any more uncertainty, but in an absolutely uncertain territory like that in which we move in this global pandemic we are trying to cast the greatest possible certainty. If not, what are we doing with different groups that are affected as a result of this economic closure, what are we doing with the eventual workers who are on the street today but are going to receive a benefit, what are we doing with the self-employed, that 850 000 are going to receive a termination benefit, what are we doing, for example, in strengthening public procurement at state level to provide PPE to the autonomous communities? For we are trying to give certainty in an absolutely uncertain crisis, but not only for Spain but for the whole planet. Therefore, Spain is going to get ahead, I have no doubt about that. As far as Mr de Foro Asturias, Mr Martínez Oblanca, is concerned, I am not surprised by your speech, although I am naturally grateful for your support. And to Mr Guitarte, who has made an appeal to territorial cohesion, to which I have previously referred, and has also made an appeal to something important, to unity, I would like to say that of course you are invited and convoked to these great agreements that I hope can materialize within a short time. And in this area of unity, I would like to recall, as some spokesman did, the words of the Director-General of the World Health Organization himself who spoke that the first thing was national unity. And Mrs Lastra, I would like to thank you for the support of the Socialist Group. It is indeed the moment of politics, and of politics as an agreement, that is true. We have the bad fortune, I don't know why, that when the People's Party is in the opposition we can never reach state agreements, it has to be when the Socialist Party is in the opposition. But, well, the time will come for political debate on what has happened and how we have to look to the future, and logically we will have to say many things, too, but obviously if after 2008 we were very clear that if we want growth to be sustainable we have to ensure that it is also redistributed, today that logic and that evidence is even greater. If we do not have redistribution through a strong welfare state, we will be very vulnerable to phenomena that have a lot to do with globalization, as is precisely this pandemic. Mr. Rufian said something I share. Pandemics have occurred in many places, in Africa a lot, annually, but not on the scale of the pandemic that we are living in each and every country. But, indeed, it is also a cure of humility for the West. This represents for us a cure of humility because no one had foreseen that a pandemic could spread in this way as accused and as intense in an advanced and developed society as Western society is. I do believe that somehow we are in a kind of war; I do, ladies and gentlemen. I believe this because we are talking about a global crisis; I believe this crisis has no precedent - I said earlier that we could have as a reference point the year 1918 - I believe this because of the extraordinary mobilization of economic, social and human resources that we are seeing in all areas, also on the front of research - I hope they will be successful soon - and I believe this also because nothing is going to be the same. Therefore, in that scheme it is more than obvious that we need the unity and loyalty of all political forces. The time for reproach and criticism will come. Therefore, resistance, unity and the spirit of victory. Ladies and gentlemen, I'm done. I have listened to all the speeches, as I said before, with interest. I assure you, I have taken note of all your suggestions. Those who vote in favour of the Government's proposal to extend the alarm decree are grateful on behalf of the Government and, I dare say, also on behalf of all Spaniards who, in the majority, overwhelmingly majority, support these extraordinary measures for an exceptional moment. And the answer to criticism, more or less well-founded, more or less virulent, is the same: we are all united by stopping this virus. So, unit. I sincerely believe, and I have believed for a long time, since before this emergency began, that some of you are afraid of the agreement. The word "agreement" is called and it seems that we are beginning to put epithets: this is a decoy, this is a trap, this is an occurrence. They are afraid of the agreement when they do not rule, when they do not have the strength to guide, or to command, because what they do is not guide, it is command. But we are more of those who understand otherwise the politics and the exercise of public representation. As I said at first, ladies and gentlemen, seeking the agreement would show its strength, but also its virtues. With the agreement they would also make visible their attitude, an attitude of being an example for citizens not only by respecting the state of alarm, but also by proposing solutions to reduce time to the virus to the extent that we save lives. So all I ask, ladies and gentlemen, is what I said at the beginning of my intervention, unity and loyalty. On March 25, he invited them to reflect at a time when citizens have the focus on each and every one of their representatives. Make the effort, I ask you; do it for all the Spaniards following the example of the effort they are making daily in hospitals, on the roads, in supermarkets, in the streets, in their homes. Think for a moment about what is happening in the world and the reading that Governments and opposition are doing in other parliaments of the world, not far from here, in the world, ladies and gentlemen; in the world. Make that effort for this country you love so much, they say. Because here there are only two options: to overcome the crisis with agreements, with constructive proposals and demands within the framework of unity and solidarity or to be the only country in the world whose opposition offers as the only alternative the controversy by failing to rise to the greatest health emergency in the history of our democracy. In the first option, ladies and gentlemen, you will have all my thanks, all my respect and all my support. In the second, as President of the Government in a twice-extended state of alarm, all I have to do is re-place them to the agreement, to the courage to be up to it, as are millions of people in our country from their homes, from their hospitals, from our roads or from our markets. Show your strength and your virtues, ladies and gentlemen. Be faithful reflection of the best of Spain. Be that mirror in which all the Spanish and all the Spanish are recognized. There is a great country outside this Chamber, ladies and gentlemen, waiting for solutions and proposals, for which more time needs to be saved. There is a great country outside this Congress that hopes not to wait any longer. Thank you. [[Applauds]]

Secondly, as far as social measures are concerned, you, Mr President, must know that at this point the government's famous social shield has holes and that those holes have to be covered. It has holes in tenants that are less protected than its renters: it guarantees first the right of the renter to collect than that of the tenant to receive aid; it has holes in autonomous ones that cannot receive aid; it has holes in people who are trying to ask for the moratorium on mortgages, because it turns out that the banks ask for such a number of papers and procedures that in the end have been hosted around 10%; and it has holes in unemployed or precarious ones that are not receiving any benefits. That is why we were talking to him about a quarantine income or an emergency basic income, which is not the same as a minimum income for the most vulnerable people. Some of the Government's difficulties in moving the minimum income forward may have to do with the fact that control has to be done before, not after. There are many economists, even liberal economists, who are defending to get a quarantine income, an unconditional basic income, whose control is done ex post, then, through taxes, over who has charged how much, in what cases was deserved and who has something to return. That is faster, more effective and, from a progressive point of view, there is not going to be a situation like this, of greater social need and collective understanding of the Spaniards than at present. With regard to the question of Europe, I agree with you that the European Union is playing its part in this. If there is a European Union, that is what it is for. But also the need to address a Marshall plan in the European Union is an opportunity for the European Union to be not only a monetary union, but a fiscal union, a social union and to fulfil some of the historical functions for which we were told that it was in progress. And what we're saying here is that, first of all, you can't leave Italy alone. We believe that in the management of aid in the European Union, the countries of the South always suffer a certain, say, benevolent racism. We are not in a position to ask for help because we have wasted. In fact, we are weaker because we fulfill all the obligations of the troika that made us cut and that today make us weaker. Therefore, we must not accept any conditionality. The European Green Party, in my opinion very rightly, has proposed a billion euros in progress for Eurobonds who share their debt in Europe. I do not know what the position of Germany and Holland will continue to be, but, if they do not move forward, here is our proposal: head up a Mediterranean bloc that starts to pool debt between countries, and we are many more already - read an editorial in Der Spiegel saying the same - those that we are about to move towards a European Union of solidarity. If Germany and the Netherlands continue to block, you will lead a Mediterranean bloc that will pool debt and help us in economic reconstruction. The last question, at full speed, on the subject of the Moncloa Pacts. I understand that these are moments of uncertainty and everyone is looking for references or things that make them feel safe. It's okay to study the past. I have not moved, I have never been even among those who exalted the Transition and seemed to them that everything was fine or among those who seemed to them that everything was fatal. What I have are doubts as to whether that metaphor serves anything. Historian Xavier Domènech wrote yesterday or today an article in which he said that the situation is a little different, first of all because then it was about fighting inflation, containing wages and adjusting to the bulk of the popular classes and today it is about the opposite, starting the engine of the economy, mobilizing more public investment, increasing the disposable income of households and helping a national reconstruction with social justice. Do not be frightened - I finish this, Madam President - by the cries of the right, because they fulfil a status quo function. And it's not what they shout, it's that they scream and exaggerate so much that, almost without moving, it seems that things are revolutionary. And, of course, alongside these gentlemen, one practically stands still and is already a communist social government. The fundamental problem is not that the Spaniards are not worried about whether they see politicians in a family photo; they are worried about whether they have rent, whether they have jobs, whether they have decent public services. And that doesn't require a family photo, there are already social and institutional conditions for a big deal to move forward in national reconstruction with social justice, and for that, if they move forward, they know they're going to find us. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES_2020-04-09-CD200409.u76	We continue with the agenda. Validation or repeal of Royal Decree-Law 11/2020 of 31 March, which provides for the adoption of additional urgent measures in the social and economic fields to deal with COVID-19. To present the Royal Decree-Law, the third Vice-President of the Government and Minister for Economic Affairs and Digital Transformation has the floor on behalf of the Government.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-04-09-CD200409.u77	Thank you very much, President. Good afternoon, everyone. First of all, I would like to convey from here all my affection and solidarity to the victims of this epidemic, to their families and to their friends. I would also like to convey my gratitude and encouragement to all the people who are allowing us to move forward, making it possible, within the exceptionality in which we find ourselves, to maintain a life as normal as possible in the circumstances. I turn again to the House, as almost two weeks ago, to call for the support of the groups for a new royal decree-law adopted by the Government to implement measures in the social and economic field with which to deal with the impact of the crisis caused by COVID19. This is a real decree-law that comes to complete the safety net that we have been knitting from the very beginning to ensure the protection of citizens, particularly the most vulnerable, and to support as much as possible the business productive fabric and employment while this hibernation phase lasts. The objective, as you all know, is to ensure that our economy and our society remain in the best conditions possible during this exceptional time, so that when we finally enter the phase of descalation of the country's containment and recovery measures we have a good basis on which to reactivate ourselves. To this end, it is essential to complete the protection of our citizens, businesses and workers, to resolve some of the issues identified for the implementation and improvement of the implementation of the measures previously adopted and also to ensure the most efficient functioning of the public administration. [[The lady]] As I explained when I defended the previous package of economic and social measures in this House, since the COVID began to expand around the world and became a pandemic, the Government has been taking swift, effective measures, guided by the recommendations of health experts and aligned with what countries in our environment are doing. The measures have sought, first and foremost, to contain the health crisis, to preserve the lives of our compatriots and to avoid the collapse of our National Health System, which is demonstrating a great strength and a great quality and capacity. It is clear that the flood of income in hospital emergencies concentrated in just a few weeks has been a major challenge for the whole system, so it has been essential to strengthen it with more human and technical means, something in which we have made our commitment a priority. We have also acted at the forefront of research, facilitating and enhancing investments in the search for treatments and vaccines for this disease, because this is - we all know - the only way to solve the situation effectively and lastingly in the medium term. A fundamental part of health action has also been the measures of social containment and distancing supported by the declaration of the state of alarm and which are essential to curb the curve of contagion and thus relieve the pressure on our health system. However, it is clear that these containment measures also have a cost, an economic and social impact, especially since the widespread ban on movements, except for essential workers, which started a little over a week ago and which aims to slow down the contagion by lowering mobility. It is the most important indicator for this evolution of contagion, but I insist on this concept: it is about reducing mobility and not paralysing economic activity. In fact, hundreds of thousands of our fellow citizens continue to work not only in essential services but also in their homes, making a great effort to continue contributing to overcoming this crisis. If economic shock first came through the global value chains by reducing Chinese workforces and production capacity, the globalization of the epidemic accentuated the difficulties in supply chains and affected export demand, particularly in the field of tourism. The adoption of decisive and firm containment measures in Spain and the majority of European countries has ended up generalising the disruption of economic activity; that is, the health crisis and containment measures are already having a direct impact on the economy and society, on the productive chains and on the day-to-day lives of citizens. And as with the expansion of the virus itself, the impact of containment measures on the economy is also exponential, not linear, so that the longer they remain in force, the greater the economic and social impact. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, it is essential to tackle the health crisis as soon as possible and to avoid a collapse of the health system, as this is the only way to ensure an early recovery with the least possible economic and social damage. In other words - and I often say this - there is no dichotomy between protecting health and protecting the economy, they are both sides of the same coin, because we will not achieve it without the other. This explains and justifies the roundness of the measures we have taken from the very beginning, the decision to limit the mobility of workers in our country to the maximum in order to respond to the health need, to advance the end of the exceptional situation and to avoid entering a longer stationary situation in which we cannot deal decisively with economic recovery and have more structural damage to our productive fabric and employment. And in parallel with these health measures, this explains the roundness, the decision with which we have also acted in the economic sphere. It is essential to make it easier for businesses and workers to hibernate the activity, which is why the retrievable paid leave that the Minister of Labour is going to defend in a moment and the flexibility that we have granted to some sectors to ensure that they can reactivate once we leave this exceptional situation behind. In this context, in the scene of the packages of actions in the face of this crisis that we have been adopting for weeks and that have been validated by this House, we now add the real decree-law that we bring here today, which, as I told you before, completes the security network that we have been weaving among all of us in the face of the economic and social consequences of the crisis. The priority objectives of this royal decree-law remain the same as the last time I was here on the podium: to strengthen the struggle in the health field, to support citizens, the self-employed, the workers, especially the most vulnerable, and to facilitate the maintenance of the productive fabric in order to avoid a more lasting impact on economic activity and employment. That is why, since the Third Vice-President of the Government and Ministry of Economic Affairs and Digital Transformation, we have coordinated the action of up to fourteen ministries to illuminate this rule, which includes more than fifty initiatives, including the extension of the previous exceptional measures until one month after the end of the declaration of state of alarm. But in addition to this extension we abound on several fronts in response to requests from the social partners. We are also trying to introduce technical improvements to ensure the effectiveness of the measures and at other times what we do is to try to respond to requests from collectives that had not been covered by the actual previous decree-laws. As I told them, supporting vulnerable workers, families and groups to alleviate their financial situation and their fixed costs and ultimately to be able to cover this exceptional situation as best as possible is a priority for the Government. That is why a first set of measures is aimed at the most vulnerable families and groups. First, its suspending evictions due to non-payment of rent in the usual housing in vulnerable groups without housing alternative for a maximum period of six months. In addition, the autonomous renewal of rental contracts that expire within two months of the end of the state of alarm for a period of also six months is agreed and the terms and conditions that existed are maintained in force. This royal decree-law also approves a moratorium on the payment of rent by maintaining an adequate balance between the interests of owners and tenants. For this we distinguish between public owners and large housing owners and, on the other hand, small owners, so that in the case of public owners and large tenants, those who have more than ten urban buildings or more than 1500 square meters built, will have to apply a 50% reduction in rent to the tenant or a moratorium of up to four months in the payment of rent to vulnerable tenants. Once this situation has been overcome, tenants will return unpaid contributions over a period of at least three years without interest or penalties. On the other hand, where the lessor is not a large estate holder, which happens in 85% of the cases, tenants may request a postponement in the payment of their rent. In the event that an agreement is not reached with the lessor, a new programme of transitional zero-cost financing aid is available for these households in difficulty, which will be granted by banks with the approval of the State through the Official Credit Institute. These are final loans for the payment of rent, for up to six months, to be returned within a period of up to ten years. In this way, we prevent the problems of vulnerable tenants from being transferred to small owners, whose income can depend precisely on the income of their properties. In addition, a new rental aid programme with EUR 700 million is being launched. The standard also incorporates a specific programme for victims of gender-based violence, homeless persons and other particularly vulnerable persons. The mortgage moratorium is extended until three months, in terms we had already approved a few weeks ago, but extending the protected collective. This allows self-employed and small-scale owners to benefit from the moratorium, not for their usual housing, as set out in the previous Royal Decree-Law, but also for the loan that weighs on their commercial premises, and we clarify how to apply this measure so that the suspended quotas are not liquidated after the expiry of the period of the moratorium but that all future payments are postponed for an equivalent period. We also include the costs associated with supplies for the purpose of identifying vulnerable families and facilitate the accreditation of this vulnerability situation through the submission of a responsible declaration. Local corporations are also empowered to use the surplus from previous exercises to take measures to support vulnerable housing groups. On the other hand, to ensure that citizens are not excluded from the financial system, the moratorium on payments is extended to non-mortgage loans and loans, including consumer credits in particular. The protection of the most vulnerable citizens in relation to their energy supplies is extended by extending the electric social bond to new collectives, such as self-employed ones who have ceased their activity or who have seen their income reduced by 75%, and it is permitted to update the regulated prices of the butane candy and the Last resort price of natural gas, which were frozen, as you will all remember, by a previous royal decree-law. The review is allowed provided it is low and benefits consumers. It is also prohibited to cut energy or water supplies in the usual housing for any reason other than security of supply. This extends the scope from which we had previously established that it was only for vulnerable groups. Also with a view to alleviating the lack of liquidity in households, the cases in which pension funds can be rescued by persons who are receiving unemployment benefit as a result of temporary employment regulations, employers in an establishment whose opening has been suspended by the declaration of the state of alarm or self-employed persons who have ceased their activity as a result of COVID-19 are extended. Contracts for the sale of goods and the provision of services, whether or not on a successive basis, the execution of which has become impossible, allowing consumers and users to settle them within 14 days, also by paralysing the collection of new fees until the services are resumed and, in the case of services involving several suppliers, such as, for example, combined travel, a refund or a voucher may be chosen between a refund or a voucher to be used within one year of the end of the state of alarm. With regard to the ban on telecommunications portability that we decree a few weeks ago, the rule is set out to establish that it only affects the operations in which the physical presence of operators or the user is necessary to focus the restriction on those areas that have to do with mobility and, therefore, with the infection of COVID. For workers receiving care allowance for minors affected by cancer or other serious diseases, it is decreed that during the state of alarm their allowance will not be affected by temporary employment regulations. And finally, in order to avoid the intensification of online gambling consumption and to protect, above all, minors and people with gambling disorders in this exceptional situation of confinement in their homes, the advertising of operators in this sector is limited. In the spirit that has guided us at all times not to leave anyone behind, measures are taken to care for the collective of household employees, who are in a situation of great vulnerability due to the particular characteristics of their work or their contribution scheme and the impact of the measures taken to contain the demand for their services. And for them a new temporary special allowance is created, which will cover cases of lack of activity, reduction of hours worked or termination of the contract. The same applies to temporary workers whose contracts are extinguished during these weeks and who are not entitled to the benefit. For them a new subsidy is created, so that they do not run out of income while this situation continues and until they can start looking for a new job. As you can see, the cluster of measures directed directly at citizens and, in particular, at the most vulnerable is the main body of this royal decree-law. A second package of measures is targeted at businesses and the self-employed, a group which is also a priority for all of us and which have been targeted at a large number of measures in the various packages that we have adopted in recent weeks, including, as you know, the exceptional benefit for cessation of activity or reduction of income, the postponement of tax payments, liquidity support lines, etc. Now, along with some of the initiatives that I have already mentioned that can already benefit, such as the mortgage moratorium, the electric social bond, it is also enabled by this royal decree-law to Social Security to grant a six-month moratorium on the payment of social contributions, both to businesses and self-employed; it is allowed to self-employed enterprises and workers to postpone the payment of debts to be paid between April and June that they have with Social Security, with a reduction in the required interest rate; and it is also given to self-employed persons a interest-free moratorium on the payment of contributions for the days actually worked in March. Repayment obligations for loans granted by autonomous communities and local authorities to entrepreneurs and self-employed persons are also extraordinarily deferred. Fifthly, the extraordinary cessation benefit is adapted to cover the situation of certain sectors which have a high degree of seasonality, such as agriculture, culture, or those linked to events such as Holy Week or others; among them, as an anecdote, I would like to share with you that we have received this request in particular from the cut flower sector, which is clearly very affected by the exceptional situation and by the cancellation of the ceremonies that usually take place at this time of year. Specific treatment is also given, in relation to the maintenance of employment, to sectors with enormous labour volatility or in which the volume of workers can depend substantially on a given benefit or event, such as the performing arts, music, cinema or audiovisual sector, to adequately collect the specificities they may have. Certain measures are also included to make contracts and supplies more flexible for self-employed persons and businesses, both electricity and natural gas, and are even allowed to suspend payment of their invoices, all of which are designed to alleviate and improve the financial situation of these groups. On the other hand, a third block of this royal decree-law is aimed at supporting economic activity and industry, particularly by ensuring the liquidity of our productive fabric during this exceptional period. As you all know, the first step along this path has already been taken with measures such as guarantees from the Official Credit Institute, up to EUR 100 billion to cover circulation, pay invoices or meet other concepts of self-employed persons and companies; guarantees that have begun to be granted this Monday, following the signing of the relevant agreements with financial institutions, and that will cover operations granted since 18 March with a retroactive application so that there is no gap or gap in the liquidity guarantee. The first tranche activated, of EUR 20 billion, has been set aside at 50% to guarantee loans to self-employed and SMEs, and both the Government and the Bank of Spain are very vigilant to ensure that the conditions laid down are met, in particular that the benefit derived from the public guarantee is transferred to customers in the form of lower interest, longer term or longer period of deficiency, that the approval of these loans is not conditional on the procurement of any other service or product, and that all customers' lines of circulation are kept open until 30 September. I can tell you today that, according to the latest data this morning, some 33 000 operations have been incorporated into the ICO platform, worth more than EUR 28 billion in guarantees, and the objective of prioritising the situation of self-employed people and SMEs is also being met. For this group, operations totalling almost EUR 27 billion have already been approved. In parallel with the absorption of this first section of the collateral line, we are preparing the mobilization of the second section, reserved entirely for autonomous and SMEs, to ensure that liquidity continues to flow adequately towards the productive tissue. As announced this morning by the President of the Government in the rostrum, tomorrow we will approve the launch of this second tranche of the endorsement line. In addition to this tremendously important measure from the quantitative point of view of the collateral line, with this royal decree-law that we bring to validation today we take a step further by activating new measures that will ease the potential liquidity tensions of companies in our country. Among other things, the system of guarantees and advances is amended to speed up and facilitate the management of all aid from the General Secretariat of Industry and Small and Medium-sized Enterprises to facilitate refinancing. The provision of the Technical Provision Fund of the Spanish Company for Reaffirmation, Cersa, is also increased by EUR 60 million to provide an extraordinary coverage of the credit risk in financing operations, so that it can assume about EUR 1 billion of risk that will enable 2000 million to be mobilised, thus benefiting some 20 000 SMEs and self-employed people. The amount paid by companies at events organised by ICEX that have been cancelled shall be refunded and additional aid shall be granted for incurred expenses not recoverable at other international events. The payment of interest and repayments of loans granted by the Secretariat of State for Tourism under the various Entrepreneurship programmes is suspended for one year, in order to ensure the liquidity and viability of companies in one of the most hit sectors, such as tourism. The CDTI is included among the bodies that can speed up aid and contributions to the business sector to promote innovation in the fight against the pandemic. The viable competing companies are also allowed to accept ERTE so that they do not end up in liquidation because of the exceptional situation generated by COVID, provided that they subscribe to the commitment to maintain employment for six months after the end of the state of alarm. In addition, the standard includes other more technical cutting measures, e.g. an exceptional period to allow the marketing of winter gasoline. The extension of permits for access and connection to electricity networks to give legal certainty to electricity generation projects, on which substantial investments are required to meet our renewable penetration targets, which will be reactivated as soon as we put an end to this exceptional state of affairs. An exemption for electricity and natural gas traders to liquidate VAT, excise duty on electricity and excise duty on hydrocarbons, as well as to pay tolls on access to transport and distribution networks associated with users who suspend their payments, or an exceptional and transitional temporary compensation for certain compulsory population coverage costs of the State-wide digital terrestrial television service so that companies can continue to provide that essential service. There is also another set of technical improvements to solve problems and ensure the proper and effective functioning of public administrations. For example, public sector accountability periods are allowed to be suspended, donations are facilitated, the possibility of postponing tax debts to the customs area for six months without interest being accrued for the first three months. The time limits for different administrative procedures of companies are further relaxed. The Government is entrusted with the development of a plan to strengthen the social and administrative courts, as well as the commercial courts, when the state of alarm is lifted to deal with the foreseeable increase in activity that they will have in the coming weeks. Special rules are laid down for the duration of certain contracts of teaching and research staff concluded by universities to facilitate their access to new contracts or calls. Specific provisions are applied for contracts of employment signed in the field of research and staff employed in the National Health System, including the compatibility of the retirement pension with the appointment as statutory staff of health professionals so that they can be reinstated in the active service at this exceptional time, and the voluntary collaboration of all public employees on the health front is facilitated. The resources for vocational training are allowed to be applied to the financing of any of the benefits and actions of the unemployment protection system. Banking foundations are granted an extension of up to two years within the period of divestment and disposal in exchange for providing a reserve fund, and in terms of investment the National Securities Market Commission is empowered to modify the requirements applicable to companies managing collective investment institutions. In short, ladies and gentlemen, it is a tremendously complex and comprehensive package of measures that also cover a wide range of specific situations of people, small and medium-sized enterprises, self-employed, in order to complete this safety net for society as a whole. This package represents a very important strengthening of the financial capacity of businesses, the self-employed and our families, which is in addition to the more than EUR 100 billion of mobilization of additional public expenditure, liquidity and guarantees deriving from the real decree-laws already approved earlier. It is clear that we are making a very important effort as a society, and I believe that it is also clear that the individual effort of countries will not be enough to overcome this situation. This is a crisis that transcends national borders, affects all countries in one way or another and requires a response at international level and specifically from the European Union. There is a need for greater coordination and for the tools to enable us to finance recovery jointly, in a European way, in order for the Union to honour its name. As you know, the Spanish Government is very attentive to the evolution of events; we are listening to the requests coming from the various groups, appreciating closely and exchanging views with the social actors and the rest of the political formations, and taking decisive steps to meet the different needs, with a strong and irrevocable commitment not to leave anyone behind and with the view set to overcome the health emergency as effectively as possible, so that we can enter as soon as possible into the new phase of descalation and transition towards the recovery that you have been talking about this morning in this Congress of Deputies. And, in this regard, on the European issue - and with this I conclude the presentation - I think it is important to share with you the positive assessment of the firmness with which the European Central Bank has taken decisions in recent weeks; also our positive assessment of other movements, such as the European Commission's proposal to create an unemployment reinsurance system - an issue that the Spanish Government has been advocating since the first day - and also the flexibility of the rules of the Stability and Growth Pact. But positive measures are not enough; a more determined, more European common response is needed, and to achieve this is what we have been working on since the first day. Today, as you know, I will be participating in a new meeting of the Eurogroup, at which I hope we will adopt a safety net for businesses, states and workers throughout the Union; a network of approximately EUR 500 billion in loans through institutions such as the European Investment Bank, the European Stability Mechanism or the Community budget itself. This short-term liquidity package would be a positive first step in articulating a joint response. From our point of view it is a positive first step, but it is not enough. We need to work in parallel with a medium-term perspective to create a debt-sharing mechanism. The exit from this crisis cannot assume that each country will assume independently, in isolation, the fiscal burden that will arise both from the measures we are taking at this time to respond to the health, economic and social crisis and from the investment plan that will be indispensable to be able to address and boost economic recovery when we move to the next stages. I therefore conclude with this idea. No one can escape that we will still have to face difficult weeks of great uncertainty, which will require patience in order not to lose control of the situation and continue to keep the virus at bay, but I am convinced that Spanish society will continue to act with exemplaryness, temperance and solidarity, as it has been doing since the beginning of this crisis. I have heard many times that Spain is a great country with a lot of assets and, among them, I always praise the creativity, solidarity, adaptability and resilience of our people. And I think this crisis is showing us all that we have a lot of reason to be proud of our country; first of all, for the citizens, who are individually and selflessly helping those who need it most every day, giving encouragement from the balconies and windows, thanking openly and sincerely for the effort of all those who do not let the country stop; but we also need to be proud of the workers who in complicated circumstances come to their jobs to ensure our health, continue to produce our food and allow us to put food on the table, keep our cities clean, preserve our security, operate critical infrastructures, disinfect common living spaces and allow us to hold this debate today. And here I would also like to include, let me say, the public workers, who make the administration continue to function at such necessary times, as well as all those who are developing their activity from home. The Minister of Labour, who is already here with us, knows well and lives every day the intense work that officials are having to do in those ministries that have to implement the economic and social cutting measures that we propose and that you have been validating in this Parliament. And this, supporting citizens, workers, businesses, this is what we do with this royal decree-law and with the set of measures that we have adopted so far, we build a safety net that protects our economy, that protects our society in this transitional and exceptional phase and that allows us to approach these weeks, strengthen our resilience, in the hope that we will soon be discussing the plans for reactivation and momentum for our country here. But in order for that moment to arrive as soon as possible, we have to row together in the same direction thinking of those who need us most, doing the same thing as the Spanish citizens because I think we are all clear that we will only get out of this crisis quickly if we come out together. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

They include, for example, the ban on eviction of tenants for six months, as has already been said. This includes the moratorium on rent debt or the line of guarantees for the granting of financing aid without costs or interest to the applicant, or the direct granting of rental aid itself, in which for the first time the category of large holders is introduced in Spain, something that has been claimed here in this House, because we cannot ask the same sacrifice from a family that owns two, three houses than from the fund that owns a huge real estate capital; this seems like a drawer. Similarly, the payment of loans or loans without mortgage guarantee to vulnerable creditors is temporarily suspended. With regard to the labour market - it has also been highlighted - an extraordinary subsidy is being created for domestic workers; we are talking about women in particular, 96% of this sector. Consumer rights are also strengthened in the suspension or termination of contracts, the reimbursement of pension plans and protection against ludopathy. A social bonus is also created for self-employed persons who have ceased their activity or have seen their turnover reduced, while a moratorium on social security contributions is established. All these measures give us an idea that, in fact, the Government has chosen, and has done, unlike the last financial crisis, to defend the interests of the majority. Indeed, much remains to be done to speed up access to all these social funds, credit lines and approved moratoriums; in fact, we are aware of the difficulties that some sectors have experienced in accessing these aids designed to alleviate the unbearable burden that all this entails for vulnerable families, self-employed and workers. That is why we from our parliamentary group continue to bet on the creation of a vital minimum income, in line with what other governments are already proposing; that is why we have also loyally sent the Government some proposals to simplify procedures and thus speed up the arrival of these aids. This brings me to the second of the tautologies, repeated in unison by the corifes of the right: that of which this government is improvising. What one has to listen to! At a time of crisis, completely alien to the very productive model, completely unpredictable, in which every day counts to save thousands of lives, is not the Government, any Government, forced to improvise? In fact, the actual decree that concerns us shows this improvisation insofar as, for example, it extends the period of suspension to three months for mortgages with respect to what had already been approved in Royal Decree-Law 8/2020, as is the extension of coverage, the moratorium to self-employed and tenants in situations due to the loss of rent, and a second phase of lines of endorsement is also implemented, since the first, of 20 billion, has proved to be insufficient. All this remains a clear symptom of improvisation, of necessary improvisation, as a requirement of effectiveness and endurance in the many complex urgent responses that are required. Let me close by introducing a second reflection, Madam Minister, on the European scale, and more now, when you are about to travel to start this second round of negotiations in Brussels; and you will forgive me for the fight metaphor, because there are countries to the north that have decided to make this a fight against the south. Specifically, we have had to listen to the Dutch government insinuations of protection against our country or against Italy; the government that has most practiced fiscal dumping in the European Union now calls us irresponsible. Certainly, the forms were quite impertinent, even clumsy I would say, but the bottom has no other qualification than that given to it by Mr António Costa, Portuguese Prime Minister: simply repugnant. Madam Vice-President, we are aware of the effort you are making personally to find some viable mechanism for debt-sharing in Europe. Perhaps the possibility of the Commission issuing its own titles and transferring resources to countries according to the needs of each might be the solution sought. In any case, we all know that it will not even be enough in the medium term with the commitment of the European Central Bank to provide almost a billion euros in order to avoid speculative attacks by way of buying national debt securities. Allow me, Minister, to conclude, a kind recommendation for this second round and for those who are to come. We will need the strategic support of the Iberian brothers: of the Republic of Portugal, of France, yes, of course, but also of Italy. We cannot afford to break that alliance. There are many reasons that I could put forward here to explain why we have to maintain that alliance, but I will simply detail one: the correlation of forces would crush us. Thank you. [[Applauds]]

The goal is not to close any company and not to lose any jobs; many have already been lost, we know, but we are going to let go of the skin to get them back. If one thing we have learned from previous crises is that defending work is a guarantee for people who keep their jobs, but also the best way, along with minimum living income, of economic recovery. The economy is people. An unemployed person who loses his or her job or an autonomous person who closes his or her activity are not simply figures for us. It's an economic problem, it's a social problem, it's a problem for our country, because when some people talk about economics they seem to reduce it to the business of a few. The measures of this Government are not intended for the benefits of a few shareholders, it is true; they are intended to revive economic activity and above all work. We therefore want the vitality shown by the Spanish economy and the good behaviour of employment not to be a portrait far from the immediate past. Today we stay in our homes, without being able to go out on the street, but we do not resign to regain our way of life. Nor do we give up reactivating our economy, creating jobs. They are not prints of the past because they are too alive still in our experience. Many of your ladies and gentlemen know my previous activity as a Member. They know that I have left my skin, and I continue to do so, defending the productive economy, defending our companies, defending our industry, defending our self-employed, and I still think this is key. Our goal and huge public resources are aimed at supporting businesses to maintain their activity and employment and contribute to the country's growth; supporting businesses to generate jobs and create wealth; supporting jobs to create wealth; and activating businesses and incomes to live decently for their work. Economic, labour and social objectives must therefore be closely linked. This crisis cannot be repaid by those who have already paid it; it would be unjust and unworthy. When we say that this virus we stop together, we are also saying that this crisis we overcome together. If you'll allow me, I'm going to report on the royal decree-laws we're asking for and urging their validation. As I said, the first of these is 9/2020 of 27 March, by which complementary measures are taken in the workplace to alleviate the effects of COVID-19. As his own name indicates, it is a complementary norm or, what is the same, a tax of the very important Royal Decree-Law 8/2020, which his lordships had the opportunity to validate on March 25 of this year and which is the key to the vault of the labor response to the crisis of the COVID-19. In this royal decree-law, in 8/2020, green light was given to the most ambitious set of employment maintenance devices in our history. The idea is simple and precise. As we fight this health crisis, companies and workers have at their disposal formulas to put their activity in parentheses, to stop on the road that allows, after overcoming the most harmful effects, to resume the activity, to return to normality. And as long as this parenthesis lasts, the public power, far from being profiled, assumes a decisive protective role for the workers, guaranteeing them unemployment, whether or not they have a period of lack, and putting, when all this happens, what we have called the accountant to zero, that is, by replacing the public unemployment benefits. For the companies, an exemption from their very ambitious contributions has been articulated, which allows them to endure, almost hibernate, to return to the scene without jams, without weights to drag. This is, as you know, a temporary employment regulation dossier, a management instrument already launched by President Zapatero in 2010, with strong European roots and which we have adapted to deal with a pandemic, transforming an ordinary measure into a protective shield for the service of health and the maintenance of employment. On the success of this measure, the following information is given: on the 9th day of yesterday, 8 April, we had communicated by the labour authorities of the Autonomous Communities a total of 450 000 ERTE, a number which in itself demonstrates the success of the measure. It should also be remembered in this House that people affected by an ERTE are busy people. Both Eurostat and all labour force surveys, including our EPA, collect it in this way. For a period of three months this is directly the case; moreover, Eurostat still considers them to be occupied after three months if ERTE continues and receives more than 50% of its salary, as is the case with the benefit we have set up. In short, ladies and gentlemen, the people affected by an ERTE are not unemployed. They have a job and they contribute for it. We should use the data rigorously, as they do in the countries of our environment. It seems to me that we already have too many people standing still; let us avoid confusing citizens. A worker affected by an ERTE is not a stopped person. Here and around the world the goal of ERTE is precisely to avoid unemployment. Let me be honest with you. I believe that we have made a certain success of this path, which could have meant an unimaginable destruction of employment; think that we would not have arbitrated this measure. I strongly affirm that this is not a triumph of this Government, it is a triumph of this country. It is a triumph of the social partners who have agreed with this Government to implement this measure. It is true that many relationships of a temporary nature have failed to incorporate them into the ERTs' suspense mechanism. We have once again been overwhelmed by the overreaction of a labour market hit by precariousness and which once again throws thousands of temporary workers into the streets in the face of the first problem. This is another teaching for when this is over. We cannot continue to maintain this level of precariousness that plagues all sectors, even those who heroically play life in the front line of hospitals. A nurse or a doctor cannot be allowed to link contracts for weeks without security and stability that will allow them to do their job well. [[Applauds]] All this determines our inability to contain the hemorrhage of job destruction in crises, unlike what happens in other countries in our environment not so exposed to the scourge of precariousness. Maintaining a temporary bag of the dimensions of Spanish, which is not compared in Europe, means that we lack effective tools to contain part of the destruction of employment when turbulence occurs. The sad fate of our country's temporary workers - most of them, by the way, linked to contracts that do not respect the law - is to be at the forefront of the unemployment caused by the crises. We therefore welcome this experience which has unfortunately been repeated so many times in our country and which explains our weakness in tackling economic problems and their impact on the labour market. In this, as in all other crises, the old resists to disappear and the new has just been born. Let's change this dynamic. Let's move on to the new thing. Let's get out of here not only by containing the destruction of employment; this is not about us getting out of here by ensuring job stability, giving our workers and our companies security, lasting and reliable links that do not allow hundreds of thousands of people in precarious lives to leave work to the first of change and stay out of the ordinary aid arrangements. They know that we have been sensitive to this situation to the point of designing, as has been said in this House, a specific unemployment benefit for temporary workers and also for domestic workers. But what we want is not to have to continue acting in an exceptional way. As you know, we arrived at the ministry very questioned and flying the flag of stability and, after this experience of the crisis of the COVID19, let me tell you that we waved it even more and if it fits more forcefully. Today is not the time, but this House, believe me, will speak again of precariousness in the future, and it will do so to choose to resolve it. This is the commitment of the Ministry of Labour and Social Economy, this is the commitment of the Government of Spain. We are certainly in a collective effort that has a well-defined mission: to put an end to the health crisis and to do so without damaging employment. And in order to strengthen this objective in which I have no doubt that all Members of the House will agree, the complementary measures of Royal Decree 9/2020 which I now defend are guided by common sense. They are, if I may, almost compulsory measures, logical deductions I dare say. I will list them and give you a brief justification for each of them. As for the first of these - look, I stop here -, today the Canarian government, the canarian labour authority, has stopped an ERTE from the most important employer of private health. Note the importance of the first of the measures contained in this royal decree, the protection of health avoiding the closure of health and social facilities. If we want to protect the health of citizens, the main objective of this Government, let us begin by mobilizing all the health and social resources available to us. It is not possible for the ERTE system, as the Canarian labour authority has done today, to be used to detract resources from the system at a time when the health of our compatriots requires every effort to be able regardless of whether that effort is public or private. This is why the first step of the Royal Decree-Law is to maintain the activity of health centres and care centres for the elderly. As I said a moment ago, unfortunately some of these centers had already expressed their intention to evade their responsibilities, which justified the prompt and immediate response of Royal Decree 9/2020, whose validation I ask you today. Prioritizing health requires everyone's contribution and it is not permissible to stop providing services when they are most needed. The second of the measures seeks to prevent the force majeure and the rest of the economic, technical, organisational and production causes that give rise to ERTEs from justifying dismissal. This is another binding consequence of the kind of turbulence we are going through. It seems clear that COVID-19 is a transitory, more or less persistent, but transitory circumstance, and that the Government's reaction to it has been to make temporary adjustment mechanisms generously supported by public funds available to both workers and companies themselves. Measures and aid for the coronavirus are to avoid redundancies; it makes no sense to receive aid and destroy employment. Let me also draw on my experience. If this had not been said by Royal Decree 9/2020, which we now validate, I am sure that the judges would have said so at the first opportunity before them. We already have a similar experience with 52 d). We are faced with a technical derivative of the extension of ERTEs rather than anything else, but we are also faced with a norm that imposes a standard of conduct, one of these norms that is said to be socially responsible. It is not the time to fire, but to endure and the state is a key pillar that will do everything in its power to help companies and workers also endure. Let me be clear, we will not tolerate an irresponsible minority, an irresponsible minority taking advantage of this pandemic to thwart our efforts, demonstrating socially unacceptable behaviour. I know that I am addressing a few, a very few, but I want to convey a strong message to those few: this is not the time to say goodbye. And I know we're not alone in this way of acting. Our neighbours in Europe, uniquely Italy, a country, by the way, with an enormous legal-laboral tradition, which has been a constant source of inspiration for us, have followed this same path. At the time, we have gone a step further, to the extent that, as you know, Italy has adhered to the prohibition of dismissing only economic causes and we have extended it, in addition to economic assumptions, to the causes of force majeure, to organisational and production techniques. In short, we are faced with a compulsory, technically essential and socially necessary measure, which is the most important, necessary, by which we ask companies to make use of the exceptional measures decreed and - I repeat - not to fire them. This same purpose of maintaining employment is the one that justifies another of the contributions of the Royal Decree-Law. This is Article 5, which, as you know, provides for the interruption of the calculation of the maximum duration of temporary contracts. Here too, we strongly want to tackle and distance any impact of COVID-19 from the dynamics of the duration of labour relations. Temporary contracts will also enter into that parenthesis that we test as a labour response to the health crisis. Your times will resume once this crisis is over. I insist on our purpose: we want to make a parenthesis that allows us to go back to where we had left it before the COVID-19 impacted our lives. So far, the substantive measures of Royal Decree 9/2020, whose validation we intend. There are also very important additional contributions to the rule. If I may say so, these are as decisive contributions as the previous ones, for example, so that the mechanism of the temporary employment regulation dossiers will work fat and allow workers to access the unemployment benefit with agility, even in circumstances such as the current ones, which, as you know well, decimate public staffing and generally prevent face-to-face care. Something - let me express it in this Congress - that makes worthy of praise the tireless task of thousands and thousands of public employees who, beyond their obligations, are leaving their skin to quickly solve the hundreds of thousands of ERTE presented, as well as the payment of public unemployment benefits. I would like to make public in this House my appreciation and that of the Government of Spain. I would also like to express our appreciation to the labour authorities of the whole State and to all the staff of the Ministry of Labour and Social Economy, in particular that of SEPE. You are a pride for our country. The decisive measure to speed up workers' access to unemployment, the Spanish variety, is the so-called collective application for unemployment, submitted by the company to the entity managing the benefits, acting on behalf of the workers. As you are well aware, this is done by prioritizing speed, but without neglecting rigour and without penalizing the amount of benefits. I would also like to highlight the agreement reached with the financial institutions so that they can anticipate the payment of unemployment, thereby alleviating the financial burdens of the workers, without waiting for the regular payment of the payroll by SEPE, which, as you all know, always takes place on the 10th of each month. In turn, we have offered agility to the world of the social economy so that they can benefit from ERTE even if they lack the appropriate means to hold virtual meetings of their general assemblies, depositing in these cases the decision on their governing council. Once again, protecting health and offering solutions to businesses, also those that take the form of cooperatives, so decisive for this country and that they will have to contribute so much to recovery. The impact of all these flexibility mechanisms has been extraordinary in the management developed by SEPE. Of course, such a considerable deployment of formulas that prioritize the payment of unemployment, so that no one will have a bad time during this crisis, requires, if I may, control measures. This is provided for in Royal Decree No. 9/2020 and it is provided for, as is the responsibility for responsible management of public resources. The commission of the Labour and Social Security Inspectorate, in collaboration with other administrations, to pursue fraud in collective applications for unemployment, acts as a form of closure of the system and prevents anti-social behaviour that is tempted to take advantage of the crisis in order to obtain benefits that would be inadmissible. In short, an ambitious ERTE system, which is a key part of our labour response to COVID-19; a response, as I said, of clear European roots, in line with the priority of negotiated internal flexibility and with the support of the social partners. As for the Royal Decree 10/2020, it is a rule that sets a new level, perhaps more intense, in the state of alarm. In the last week of March, all scientific recommendations revealed the need to intensify confinement, limit the movement of people during the week to a minimum, convert every day of the week to Sunday. We are now beginning to see the effectiveness of these measures. This new level of mobility restriction, which limited activity to essential services, required a more adequate and effective figure for the holiday period of Easter. The figure of the retrievable remunerated permit provides adequate coverage at the most intense time of mobility restriction, with the exception of this restriction on essential services. Royal Decree 10/2020 provides for a detailed identification of the activities excluded from the retrievable remuneration permit. The maintenance of activities essential for the community is guaranteed, and the maintenance of activities essential for the maintenance of the company itself is also guaranteed. The formula of the retrievable paid leave is simple, but it is very effective: it allows workers, whose contracts are suspended, to continue to receive their ordinary remuneration for the time they must be confined, from 30 March to 9 April. The hours corresponding to these days of inactivity form a bag of recoverable hours that will be compensated later, until December 31 of this year. This figure of recoverable work, as you know well, is not new. In the labour legal system, for example, article 5 of Royal Decree 2001/1983 was already - let us recall - and it disappeared, as you know, from the legal system because it could not be a normal mechanism for the operation of enterprises. However, in an emergency situation such as the current one and with due guarantees, it is a very appropriate instrument. Retrievable paid leave allows the cost of the health emergency to be shared between the company and the workers. On the one hand, the company must pay its full remuneration to the worker throughout April, although for a part of the same, fifteen days, it does not provide its services. On the other hand, the worker will have to be prepared to adjust his working hours at some point until 31 December this year, in order to carry out the hours that could not be worked in April by the forced confinement. In any case, the retrievable remunerated permit does not replace the ERTE which, as you know, may continue to be requested. This is a more appropriate instrument for the moment of greater limitation of mobility and which distributes this effort between workers and enterprises. In the case of this Royal Decree-Law, a mechanism is set up to ensure that the return, if I may, of the outstanding hours is carried out in an agreed manner with the representatives of the workers, and it is also ensured that the compensation must be carried out with full respect for article 34, daily rest, weekly rest, and, of course, respect for workers' rights of conciliation. With regard to the extraordinary and urgent need, the exceptional circumstances in which the royal decrees that the Government now submits to Congress for validation are well known. The normative body that is being generated in these days via royal decrees is an emergency regulation that is based on an ordinary order designed for a situation of normality, and this, as you know, has required intense changes. All the measures of this exceptional moment are destined to disappear so that, in the end, our regulatory system will be normal again, but the fact that they are very short measures over time does not remain a peak of rigour. Every measure taken, every adjustment of our system to the health emergency situation, has been adopted quickly but gradually, taking into account the evolution of the pandemic and the instructions that have been given by the health authorities. Any other performance would have been really irresponsible. The real decrees 9/2020 and 10/2020 arise at a time of negative evolution of the pandemic. They are, therefore, real emergency decrees that we could call - if I may say so - second level, immediately prior to the second quarter of confinement, the one in which the closure has been generalized to all activities that were not essential. The Royal Decree 10/2020 sets out the rules of intense second-level confinement. It establishes the need for non-service provision in non-essential activities and creates the figure of the retrievable remuneration permit. Thus, with different strategies both royal decrees guarantee the effectiveness of the confinement and that the economy and employment also recover as soon as possible after this health crisis. I want to conclude, Madam President. Yesterday the ILO office in Spain pointed out: Since the Second World War, we did not know a picture like the one we are going to see when this health crisis came to an end. The figures we are familiar with in the international context testify to this even in countries that have not adopted social confinement measures as restrictive as those of Spain. The unemployment figures have multiplied by two or three in several European countries and the International Monetary Fund itself has recalled that there has never been a global stagnation of this size, nor in the past contexts of a global financial crisis. These perspectives should not immobilize us - quite the contrary - just make us very aware of the unknown magnitude of a phenomenon for which there are no prefabricated recipes or magic solutions. No one has the heritage of good ideas, come from wherever they come from, and at this moment we need more than ever that collective flow. We have, of course, previous experiences and we have to draw conclusions from them. Not learning from what has already been lived would be the best way to make mistakes, and answers that have not worked in the past will not work in this new scenario either. For example, the indiscriminate lowering of taxes was then a dart in the heart of our most precious good, the public. What brought us back tax policies? The dismantling of public health, the abandonment of science and a weak and weak system of protection just when we needed it the most. Austerity doesn't help us either. It is the worst possible answer, in fact, because those cuts that we already know destroy the soil on which we must build, burn the earth, and squander the possibility of growing and overcoming the difficulties. This Government has accompanied its actions to the international context in which this global pandemic operates, and has done so with a great investment effort sustained in our defence of the public. We have incorporated the expert approach, we have incorporated the recommendations of international bodies, the good practices developed in our environment, the voice of the social partners and the proposals of other political parties. Here, in this sum of forces - let me say this - there is no one left, we are very aware of it and that is what has prompted Spain to be in line with the most effective responses of other countries in our environment. The mechanism of the temporary employment regulation dossiers, the maintenance of employment and the protection of workers and their families have been identified as very positive by the Union. This security network of which Spain has been a co-promotor already operates in other countries, countries - let me also point out - with presidents and presidents who share a European parliamentary group with the majority of the political parties represented in this House. The real decree-laws that we have adopted following the declaration of the state of alarm and those that we bring to this House today are a clear indication of this will that I express here, will and determination based on dialogue. The pandemic, the need for urgent responses to very complex problems, has meant that this dialogue has sometimes not been as comprehensive as we would have liked. Remember that we have been busy raising the weekly tables of social dialogue that we had, although this week they have recovered with normality. I have already said this before, but we do not forget for a moment that the answers are in the ideas and contributions of this society as a whole, a society, the Spanish one, represented here, which has given in these days the greatest of the lessons for its cohesion, its solidarity and its courage in facing a situation as difficult as it has never been before for everyone. A few days ago the Secretary General of the United Nations, António Guterres, proposed a plan to save lives, but also to curb the hemorrhage of unemployment, and said: 'Only if we forget the political game and unite, if we understand that it is the human species that is at stake, can we overcome this'. In the big crises, Balzac said, the heart breaks or is short. I have the feeling that each and every one of us has kept our disquiet and doubts in our individual hearts and have come to the call of that greater and collective heart which is the common good and the greatness of the public; a heart that is now beating in the hands of a citizen who is acting with exemplary responsibility; a heart that is beating in the effort of our health personnel, which is guaranteeing the lives and future of our country; a heart to which this Government is going to infuse with all possible effort and determination to maintain its rhythm and vitality. I'm convinced of it. We are strong because we lean on each other and that is our engine, our impulse. Let the state act with the same height of vision as our society and let us do it together and together, because only together will we stop this pandemic, only collectively, from the public, thinking about the people of this country, we will overcome the difficulties. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
