And since we are a responsible and serious group, what we will do next is to review the alternative measures proposed by VOX and which would lead Spain to a better future. Let us be clear, this government does not believe in freedom, this government does not intend to promote prosperity, and it is not easy to understand why, but some of you have grown in hatred and resentment towards those who create jobs and the workers who prosper, perhaps because the only thing you, the lounge communists, believe in is 'expropie', and what better way to expropriate the Spanish economy than to lead it first to ruin, then to rescue the Spaniards with generous payments of basic universal income from the state. And on top of it they claim to be supported. You are like pirates who sink a great ship in good condition and then pretend to thank the shipwrecks for throwing them a lifeboat. Unfortunately, you who have grown up with great contempt for early-morning Spain, who have never visited a productive centre or on the school excursion, who hate those who are striving precisely to keep you with their taxes, you are the ones who are winning the battle to Mrs Calviño; and look at how sorry I am for her, because we can disagree in many things, but at least we have a certain respect for her and I wish her more triumphs than defeats in the following councils of ministers. I don't think it's that much to be desired by those who share a table with her in the cabinet, nor are they loyal or discreet. That is why all Spain knows that with these royal decrees Pablo Iglesias won and Mrs. Calviño lost, and with her any hope of sensibility was lost in a government, already without compromises, communist. One day, soon, the socialist base will do as its party's veteran leaders already do, it will ask Pedro Sánchez to account for why the old Spanish Socialist Workers' Party has surrendered and been dragged away by the inexperience, ignorance and ineptitude of the Adamists who have nothing solid to contribute to a government and do add so much risk to the mere survival of our economy. The president's confession was true but incomplete: with Pablo Iglesias in the government he would not sleep quiet in the Moncloa, but he could not sleep quiet productive Spain either, because the same night of the approval of these royal decrees Iglesias ordered the government to take a 180-degree turn that irreparably condemns thousands of companies, SMEs and self-employed to the closure of their businesses, hundreds of thousands of unemployed workers and Spain to address a recovery with the dry productive sector. The last time we had the opportunity to debate in this House I urged them to prioritize the defense of our productive fabric, I told them that their survival depends on how we get out of this crisis; that we get out of the crisis without a SME, without a job less will depend on our ability to recover. That is why, in my last intervention, I insisted on ensuring liquidity and employment. Firstly, liquidity, before the lack of circulation would result in the closure of companies. He told them from this rostrum that it is pointless to remove water from a plant to water it again once it is already dry, what it is about is that it does not dry. And what have you done? Instead of doing as we ask and canceling the payment of taxes and social contributions in order to reduce to the maximum the cost of some SMEs that have already stopped obtaining income, they have chosen otherwise; they have maintained their tax increase and, at the same time, they have banned the dismissals - or at least announced it - but without helping in any way the companies that you require to continue to pay the salaries of employees who are obliged to lock themselves in their homes; that is, by bringing bankruptcy to SMEs that will not be able to bear it financially and, thus, to unemployment of full staff when, in other circumstances, at least part of the jobs would have been saved. Unfortunately, we already know the result because there is already a part of that productive tissue, a part of that plant, that has dried up. With regard to the crisis, 122 000 employers in Spain, 122 000 companies and self-employed employees in charge of them have been lost; that is about 8.2% of the total number of active enterprises, according to the statistics of contribution account codes that social security produces monthly. Its promise to provide hundreds of millions of liquidity to publicly-guaranteed companies is being deployed with an exasperating sluggishness, a sluggishness that mainly stifles self-employed and small businesses; companies that demand circulation to pay their payrolls and taxes and urgently need it. The first tranche of 20 billion had a demand of twice as much as distributed; it was insufficient. And as long as those funds arrive, our companies still have to pay taxes, like those 3.2 million self-employed people who have already paid their March quota. Liquidity must be aimed at temporarily avoiding draining the resources of companies through taxes. This is how VOX moved it from the beginning and, although you have resisted, we trust that you will finally rectify it, because the postponements and moratoriums in the payment of taxes, which are not pardons, do not constitute an irreversible disorder for the state's coffers, but their payment would do so for thousands of businesses that urgently need resources to avoid closure. Secondly, I told them about the importance of maintaining employment. Spain has to emerge from this crisis with all its talent intact, and this is only achieved by avoiding dismissals while preventing the bankruptcy of companies forced to close by decree. That is why since VOX we have proposed an exceptional measure for an exceptional moment like the one we are living in: that the State assume for three months the payment of the payrolls of all those Spaniards who, as a result of the confinement decreed by its Government, cannot work - both those who have suffered an ERTE, those who have had to stay at home for the regulations that do not allow them to leave - and that the State pays a premium of 20% as compensation to the workers of the strategic sectors who are developing exemplary work during this crisis for our basic services to continue to work. And so that we have the peace of mind that all our workers, our traders, our self-employed, have a coverage during this three-month time and that we can get out of this tunnel in perfect condition and with our living companies. And it is that if for decades the Spaniards have been supporting the state, it is time for the state to support the Spaniards. Let us bear in mind that this simple measure of implementation has both the dual purpose of injecting liquidity and preserving employment, and we will not tire of repeating that liquidity and employment are the two keys to getting out of this situation. The virus that has accelerated the economic crisis that has already been seen comes from China, and it seems that in Chinese the word crisis is represented by a symbol that means danger and another that means opportunity. Well, we have to try to find what opportunity is presented to us in this crisis. We see at least two: the reindustrialization of Spain and the reform of the public administration from above. Both objectives are job generators. If we have learned something in these weeks, it is important that our supply chains are better protected. It is true that in recent decades we have outsourced production in Asia and especially in China, production of finished products and parts and components for industry. The reduction in costs was very attractive, it is true that very often based on social dumping and on a lower quality or reliability of what has been produced, but let us also not hide that Spain, like almost all of Europe, has become a bureaucratic and regulatory fiscal hell and the opportunity now presented to us is very clear. Let us make Spain move from fiscal hell to employment paradise; let us make it so attractive to establish an industry in Spain that the possible lower cost of production in other countries is offset by security in the supply chain, ease of procurement and a regulatory environment conducive to wealth creation. We compensate for our higher labour costs with greater security of supply, lower administrative costs, more facilities for creating and growing, and higher training for our workers. Let us reduce fiscal, regulatory and labour burdens, not during this crisis, but on a permanent basis. Let us at once attack the bureaucracy that slows down our progress, the same bureaucracy that in this crisis has left medical supplies paralyzed for days in customs or that has prevented the fastest distribution of aid due to lack of permits or that has shown the inefficiency of seventeen unconnected and insolidary health systems among themselves. Let us take advantage of the technology to make the Spanish administration a factor of competitiveness rather than a factor of ballast. In the same way that the Tax Agency or the Directorate-General for Traffic are prodigies of technological efficiency to raise funds from those administered, let the rest of the Administration also be so to provide services to the Spaniards. We adopt efficiency criteria in the public sector, as Spanish people have done in their personal and work fields in recent decades. While robotisation has reduced the number of jobs per production centre, it has also led to an increase in the competitiveness of Spanish companies compared to other cheaper labour-intensive countries. Let us therefore encourage that there are many more workplaces in Spain and we will once and for all destabilize hatred, suspicion and distrust of our productive sector. It is time to set priorities. If you do not do this, the economy will certainly suffer, but don't worry, we will take care of fixing it sooner than later because the Spaniards deserve it, because the Spaniards are already doing a lot for Spain and deserve that their government too will start doing something for them. In last Saturday's 'Hello President' program, Mr. Sánchez quoted John F. Kennedy. I like Kennedy a lot; I've read a lot about him and even met people around him, but let me tell you that Mr. Sánchez is not John Fitzgerald Kennedy. Our president is the anti-Kennedy because Kennedy inspired a nation by inviting them to reflect on what they could do for their country, and Mr. Sánchez by taking this quote in vain forgets that today in Spain the question is the reverse, because today it seems everyone, except Mr. Sánchez, knows what the Spaniards are doing for Spain. They are living in confinement obeying orders from the Government; they have left their jobs, with the uncertainty and discomfort that this generates, obeying orders from the Government; they have lost tens of thousands of friends and family members without even being able to give them their last farewell and have lost contact with their loved ones in hospital. The older ones had suffered in silence as progress told them that they did not matter, that they had to die to leave room for the young. Spanish health workers have worked tirelessly, without means and without the support of their rulers, putting their health at risk and on many occasions losing even their lives. The Spanish self-employed have paid their quotas or their direct and indirect taxes even when their Government has banned them from making bread in order to be able to pay those quotas. Traders have closed down their shops and entrepreneurs have had to close their SMEs. These sacrifices and many more have been made by the Spaniards, they have done so for Spain and the question today is what the government will do for the Spaniards. Ladies and gentlemen, I conclude. The right path is clear: let us preserve the economy in times of crisis, whatever it costs. We guarantee the existing jobs today and immediately thereafter. Let us work to create a legal, fiscal, labour and even cultural environment designed to generate more growth and, with this, more hope for the future for the generations to come. In this way, the prosperity generated will allow us to pay taxes with which the State sustains. [[The lady]] Madam Minister, in view of how you have been involved in this pandemic, in view of the inability of this Socialist-Communist coalition government to take appropriate measures, in view of the threefold health, economic and institutional crisis, the best thing that can happen to Spain is for you to leave and move on to a national emergency government capable of straightening out a situation that has gone away from your hands. It is no longer enough with the resignation of Pablo Iglesias, they all have to leave, because today his entire government is complicit in a drift that can bring us to ruin. And no, VOX has not come to support you on the road to ruin; VOX has come to protect Spain. Our responsibility with those who voted for us and with those who did not vote for us is to defend until the end the exercise that the Constitution has entrusted to us, the control of the Government; to defend until the end our health personnel who are having heroic behavior these days; to defend until the end the rights and freedoms of Spaniards confined to their homes, who are once again showing extraordinary maturity; and to defend our ability to promote sooner or later a recovery without a drowned SME or a lost job. Spain deserves at least one Government as good as the Spanish and therefore Spain deserves the resignation of this Government. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
ParlaMint-ES_2020-04-09-CD200409.u143	Thank you very much, Mr Espinosa de los Monteros. [[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] Thank you very much. For the Socialist Group, I have the floor, Mrs Leal Fernández.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-04-09-CD200409.u144	Thank you, President. I will try to be respectful of the times and with the patience of my colleagues in the seats. Minister, ladies and gentlemen, if we review the sitting papers on hundreds of interventions in cases of validation of royal decree-laws, you will see that a classic parliamentary debate is to discuss the extraordinary and urgent need. I don't think I'm exaggerating if I say that never as at this time was more justified and made more sense the use by the government of the royal decree-laws. Most of the dictates and validated by this House during these weeks have essentially had two major objectives: to reduce the possibilities of contagion of citizens to save human lives and to distribute the sacrifices of the crisis through the redistribution of the media. Two functions which are essential and which must always be carried out by a Government in a rule of law, but which at the moment, in the face of the brutality of the crisis we are experiencing and its consequences, are manifested as obvious, extraordinary and urgent necessity. In this scenario are framed the two royal decree-laws that we submit to validation in this item of the agenda.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-04-09-CD200409.u145	Mrs Leal, forgive me, I interrupt you for a moment to ask you again to join in the applause with our fellow citizens. [[Prolonged applause, with the House set up]] Thank you so much. Excuse me, Mrs. Leal. You can go on.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-04-09-CD200409.u146	Thank you, President. Yes, Spain acted before the rest of Europe, but, ladies and gentlemen, all the governments, all the countries in our environment - let's go back to what they want, Italy, France, Germany, Portugal or Belgium - are making decisions that are acutely affected by the emergency, surprised by the virulence of the spread, driven by the obligation to meet the peremptory needs of the sick, of the workers or of the citizens as a whole. They are governments of left or right, center, liberal or progressive, they are governments of all colors and ideologies that provide solutions, create remedies, scale measures and gradually increase their means. Everyone is doing the best they can, but let us recognize that the task is not without difficulties or obstacles. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen of the right, you could try a little in the understanding, in the understanding of the verb understand, of understanding the enormous difficulties facing this country. Yes, it is the government that directs it, but who is facing major difficulties is Spain, the Spaniards, the Spanish, the citizens on foot who are in their homes also listening to us. With this I do not mean that you cannot be demanding or critical with the Government, but if you add to the demand and criticism a dose of measure and moderation, perhaps some of the judgments and criticism that we have heard today in this House would be more equitable and proportionate, but from the loyalty and unity that the President of the Government has so much required of you. The decrees the validation of which defends my group bring cause in the statement of the state of alert of 14 March. Since then, it has been an intense pace that the Government has adopted by adopting in the Council of Ministers successive rules for the development of this state of alert. They are improving the measures imposed which are being taken, because not all measures could be taken at the same time, it was neither advisable nor possible, nor all legislative activity responds to a fixed picture, but it is a consequence of a changing and constantly evolving situation. The logic of the labour measures adopted by the Government responds to the view that the situation we are experiencing is a temporary and transitional situation, so the measures to be taken are also of the same temporary and transitional nature. The Royal Decree-Law 9/2020 is complementary to the much longer 8/2020 of 17 March, but the latter, on 17 March, condenses the basic philosophy of the Government in addressing the enormous problems that are causing the crisis of COVID-19 in the labour market. The Government's primary objective has been and is to preserve and protect jobs. This makes it easier to suspend contracts, yes, but this flexibility and facilitation of ERTE was a formula chosen by the Government and validated in this House to prevent by all means at our disposal the termination of contracts and, therefore, the destruction of employment. It is the state, in that case, that assumes responsibility, the cost of suspensions, because the government was and is aware that companies - let me say, ladies and gentlemen - also needed breathers. The State thus becomes transitional and in some way the employer of last resort. The State is the first to assume its responsibility and to take care, with the resources of all, of the difficulties of the companies. Royal Decree-Law 9/2020 also has the fundamental, albeit not unique, objective of ensuring that contracts are not extinguished, that jobs are not destroyed. The Government, ladies and gentlemen, has once again found it desirable to redistribute costs and sacrifices, and while on 17 March and for tens of thousands of workers and companies the ERTE are made possible, on 27 March it calls for an effort to anticipate companies not to extinguish contracts, not to leave anyone behind and to protect work and workers. For its part, Royal Decree-Law 10/2020, which we also bring to validation, aims to allow millions of workers to remain in their homes, helping to limit the spread of the virus to the maximum. Limitations are established, but always within the prerogatives that allow the constitutional and legal regulation of the state of alarm. Because yes, Mr Oblanca, the Constitutional judgment and jurisprudence is very clear. In Constitutional Court ruling 87/2016 on the state of alarm, if we examine strictly the legal limitations that are being decreed, we must recognize that the Government, within the extraordinary complexity of this situation, is being very careful from the point of view of respect and non-infringement of fundamental rights. In this line, which separates the limitation of the rights from the suspension of those rights, the constitutional legal content of the prerogatives provided to the Government by the declaration of a state of alarm has not been overwhelmed at any time. That is to say, ladies and gentlemen, what we validate today has a counterpart, and is that the workers remain in their homes and continue to pay their wages and, once normality has been recovered, return with their work the wages received. We therefore agree with the House that companies should continue to pay their workers for a job they are going to do in the future, if they are to validate this instrument. Someone may think that we are imposing an excessive obligation on companies at a time also very difficult for them, but it is our duty as parliamentarians - and the Government does not forget its duty - that it is time to distribute sacrifices and redistribute the means. And in the field of labour relations and the regulation of the rights and obligations of workers and enterprises, it is appropriate for the authority to exercise a tuitive power in favour of the weakest party. That is why we believe that the effort we are asking for today from companies will be returned in the future with the work of their workers. Ladies and gentlemen, unforeseen circumstances of force majeure have arisen and, as Professor Thomas de la Quadra recalled yesterday in a national newspaper, the maximum that the Government has pursued, recalling Ulpiano's alterum non laedere, was precisely that: not to harm others. It is this maxim that has led the Government to take measures that alter the ordinary limits, also the legal relations between companies and workers, for a single purpose, to limit the spread of the virus and to distribute the burdens and consequences of the crisis equitably. It's good enough to throw insidiaries and lies. From the Socialist Group we will not tire of recognizing the effort that millions of workers are making, but also tens of thousands of companies. We have both asked for sacrifices, imposed limitations and new obligations; their lives, labour relations and productive systems have been altered. All this in a very short time and with a level of compliance and acceptance that says a lot about the civic sense that this great country is going through. Workers, self-employed, employers, trade union and business organisations have been able to see and accept these and other measures with a higher interest than the individual. They have valued with a maturity that to some surprises that this is a time to tie the shoulder, to give the best of ourselves, to sacrifice us beyond one's expectations. The lesson that the whole of citizenship is giving us is the best inspiration for a time when it combines, yes, the pain and suffering of many with the courage and individual and collective sacrifice of all. In this context, we believe that these decrees are part of the reasonable and fair decisions; a necessary response, within the difficult balance in which we move. That is why we ask you to support their validation. Yes, ladies and gentlemen, our country needs, indeed, a social and economic reconstruction with guarantees to go on and on. Let's do it together; let's do it together. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
